- **#414 — [P19-B7.2c, Langston Step-2 source-finding 2026-07-01] When the future maker-entry fee flip lands, the convert-to-taker branch MUST be guarded to keep charging TAKER friction.** Today the EV kernel (`evaluateTradeExpectancy`→`getCachedCostMetrics`→`computeTotalRoundTripCost`) prices **taker both legs UNCONDITIONALLY** (`cost-model.ts:134` `fee: feeRateTaker` hardcoded; §0 "the maker-entry flip is future") — it never reads `chosen_entry_mode`/`entry_fee_rate`. So B7.2c's convert-to-taker EV re-check is correctly taker-priced *by the kernel default*, and the record's `chosen_entry_mode`→taker/`entry_fee_rate`→taker write is ACCOUNTING-only (does not reach the kernel). **RISK:** when the maker-entry flip makes the kernel mode-AWARE (reads the maker leg for a maker entry), a **maker-chosen pending that CONVERTS to taker** must still be scored at TAKER friction in the re-check — else a maker-fee-priced convert EV re-introduces the exact optimism B7.2c closed (green-lighting an EV-negative-at-taker convert → open a known loser). **★ It's an ORDERING hazard (Langston refinement), not just "keep charging taker":** once the kernel is mode-aware and reads `chosen_entry_mode`, the convert is safe ONLY if `chosen_entry_mode=taker` is written **before** the re-check reads it. If the EV re-check fires while `chosen_entry_mode` is still `=maker` (stale, pre-convert-write), a mode-aware kernel prices it MAKER → the exact optimism this closed. **The guard must FORCE taker at the re-check call site regardless of the record field** (or prove the taker-write strictly precedes the re-check read). **HOME:** the batch that lands the maker-entry kernel flip (B-4.5 §464 "maker fee ACTIVATES" / Phase-21 maker path) MUST guard the convert branch; **the test:** *a converted maker pending, mode-aware kernel — assert TAKER friction is charged EVEN IF `chosen_entry_mode` has not flipped yet at re-check time* (catches the ordering bug, not just a naive maker-read). **★ CLOSED-OVERTAKEN 2026-07-02 (P19-B7.2c as-shipped):** Kyle CUT the convert-at-timeout valve entirely (timeout = DROPPED, period — no EV re-check exists), and the marketable-at-placement fallback reads the STORED gen-time `taker_net_ev` (zero kernel re-run) — so the guarded branch this issue protects NO LONGER EXISTS in any form. Residual awareness carried forward: IF Phase-21 ever re-introduces a convert-style EV re-check alongside a mode-aware kernel, this entry's ordering analysis applies verbatim — noted in the Phase-21 resting-order item (#410's Phase-21 home). **CLOSED (overtaken by design change).**

- **#408 — [P19-B8 design discussion, Kyle question 2026-07-03 — "is there real value in systematically learning how the system trades at higher balances?" CC-B + Langston verdict: YES-but-config-not-dollars; NO second standing paper engine; HOME = ENRICH the existing 25-16 study (Kyle's own 2026-06-18 directive), don't create a duplicate.** Decomposition (3 Langston verdicts reconciled + CC-B code verification): signal GENERATION never sees balance/size (market-data only — strategies detect identically at any balance); post-B8.2-fence, DECISIONS are provably scale-free; what genuinely changes with size is (a) FILL QUALITY — **verified in code: the active paper fill is size-dependent** (`execution/depth-walk.ts` walks the real book level-by-level, VWAP over consumed levels, partial→size-down on open, beyond-depth penalty on close — the Langston crux question answered YES, so a size ladder teaches REAL friction in paper), (b) which floors bind (LPCP $25 min-notional binds at small balances, unbinds going up), (c) slot-count/sizing CONFIG options a higher balance unlocks (maxOpenPositions is a fixed 1-20 knob — Kyle clarified slots don't auto-scale; the addendum's "guardrails × balance ⇒ slots" line is RETRACTED), and (d) Kyle's mixed-size insight: the SAME balance supports different sizing policies ($150/trade vs $400/trade at $2k via risk%/max-position% settings; sizes already vary per trade via risk%÷stop-distance) — sizing-POLICY territory (↔ #399(b) fractional-Kelly). **INSTRUMENT STACK (merged verdict, stricter-wins across the three): (1) scenario-tagged CONFIG SWEEP on the PRIMARY paper env = the centerpiece** (real N-slot / per-trade-size ladder runs with genuine path-dependence, reusing the B8.2 balance-ratio-tag + calibration-exclusion machinery — contamination is a solved problem; sweep windows are scenario-tagged and excluded from live-rehearsal calibration; a sweep needs an admin/config-level scenario balance/sizing set — NOT the deleted user free-text override, name the distinction); if the fence audit finds un-convertible dollar leakage, empirical dollar-behavior climbs in value and a second env re-enters the conversation. A second standing engine buys only non-reproducible single-draw data + still-simulated fills and doubles the engine/resume risk surface — NOT justified unless the fence fails or the sweep hits a wall. Real friction at LIVE scale is learned only by the live account (launch-snap re-anchor + physics backstop, already locked). **HOME: POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP 25-16 (enriched this date with this instrument stack + fence-gating; one-home convention) ↔ 25-11 (depth-aware sizing, the crossover trigger).** Surfaced by Kyle; dug + verified by CC-B; triple-verdict reconciled with Langston 2026-07-03. **OPEN (homed: 25-16).**

- **#439 — [B-STORAGE-HARDEN Wave D OBJ-4 verification, CC-A find 2026-07-08] The xStock equity OHLC 1-minute bar channel silent-stalls and does NOT recover across app restarts — a data-integrity failure on the decision-bar feed.** During the OBJ-4 capture-cadence measurement, `xstock_spot_ohlc_1m` stopped producing bars ~14:52 UTC and stayed ~15+ min stale; each `pm2 restart` yields a brief burst of a few bars then re-stalls (observed across the 14:48 + 15:05 restarts). **Provably NOT the Wave-D throttle:** the OHLC bars held a steady ~400/min THROUGH the entire throttle=8000 window (14:32–14:47) — throttle-independent — and the stall began independent of the cadence flip; the crypto OHLC (`src=ohlc`) stayed healthy throughout; and the equity-spot TICKER feed was healthy the whole time (`connected=true`, `rows_persisted_60s ~6000`, ticker age 0–3s). So it is SPECIFICALLY the xStock equity **ohlc** channel/aggregation, not the socket. **★ Alerting gap:** the existing equity-feed silent-stall detector keys on "socket OPEN but delivered no data for 750s" — but here the socket IS delivering ticker frames, so the OHLC-specific stall is INVISIBLE to it (no alert fired for today's stall). ROOT-CAUSE candidates to dig: a Kraken-side `ohlc` subscription drop, an equity-WS `ohlc`-channel resubscription bug on reconnect, or an aggregation-writer stall. Impact: xStock strategies evaluate off these 1-min bars — stale bars would mislead/block xStock decisions when active trading is live (active trading is OFF now, so no live impact today). **HOME: a named near-term batch `B-XSTOCK-OHLC-STALL`** (data-integrity; scope = reproduce + root-cause the stall + add OHLC-specific staleness detection that the current socket-level detector misses). Surfaced + Langston/CC-B both flagged as "the bigger fish." **OPEN (homed: B-XSTOCK-OHLC-STALL).**

(c) chunk-tail wake noise: cc-wake-filter minimum-length/coalescing so sub-sentence message fragments stop waking sessions.

## ★ reorg-B4 shadow-trade layer — §13 homes (2026-06-25, gated to B9 paper-active turn-on)

> Source: `Scope Files/P19_REORG_B4_*` + `Change Lists/P19_REORG_B4_CHANGE_LIST.md` + the Step-4 Langston byte-review. Both items are DORMANT-safe today (rtb_total=0 → no shadow rows persist until paper-active turn-on ~B9); each MUST be resolved before paper-active turn-on, when shadow rows first go live.

- **#388 — Shadow rehydration fail-direction: make `rtb_shadow_pairings` authoritative on restart; quarantine ambiguous rows (never default-into-live).** Today the boot rehydration split (`vts-runner.ts rehydrateOpenVtsTrades`) routes `vts_open_trades` rows by `context.shadow === true` (strict): a shadow → `openShadowTrades`, anything else → the live `openVirtualTrades`. Langston Step-4: for a telemetry-isolation invariant, defaulting a MISLABELED shadow (a mangled jsonb boolean) into the LIVE pool is the exact contamination vector the batch exists to prevent — that row would then close through the live VTS learning cascade (outcomeFeedbackStore EMA / telemetry). The `=== true` logic is correct for LEGACY live rows (no flag → stay live), but a single jsonb boolean shouldn't be the only thing between a shadow and the learning path. **Fix:** make the persisted `rtb_shadow_pairings` sink the AUTHORITATIVE shadow identifier on rehydration — any `vts_open_trades.id` present in (open) shadow pairings is a shadow regardless of the flag; ambiguous rows quarantine, never default-into-live. **Also carries the weekend-lifecycle nicety:** the weekend bulk-suspend UPDATE (`markAllXstockWeekendSuspended`, `vts-trade-persistence.ts:190/229`) can currently match shadow xstock rows (write-path lifecycle, NOT a learning read — benign today) → exclude shadows there when this lands. **HOME: B9 (paper-active turn-on) — before the first live shadow row. OPEN (dormant-safe).**
- **#443 OPEN 2026-07-10 (CC-B, surfaced by Langston after 36h of phantom re-surfacing on alert `c4c98925`) — governance doc-gap alerts are not auto-resolved by a Langston-confirmed GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS N/A row, and NO sweep exists for alerts stuck in `acknowledged`.** **Root:** `ALERT_HANDLING_PROTOCOL.md:40` already states the intended behavior correctly — *"Acking does NOT reset the staleness clock — only `resolve` stops re-surfacing"* — so the protocol is NOT the gap. **🔴 LEG (a) AS ORIGINALLY FILED IS WRONG AND IS HEREBY WITHDRAWN — CC-B, 2026-07-10, on-record, not silently rewritten.** I filed leg (a) as *"the checker SHOULD auto-resolve a doc-gap alert when a Langston-confirmed na-skip row lands; today it doesn't."* **That feature ALREADY EXISTS, is deployed, and is correct** — verified on the live checker clone: `poller.mjs:221` in-window (`if (present || na.has(\`${batchId}:${doc}\`)) toResolveKeys.push(key)`) and `poller.mjs:394` orphan sweep (`verifyDoc = (bid,doc) => docPresent(bid,doc) || exceptions.naConfirmed.has(\`${bid}:${doc}\`)`). **I filed a fix for shipped behavior. Langston ratified it. It was hours from being built.** Had it shipped we would have added a duplicate auto-resolve, graded #443 green, reported success to Kyle — and the real defect would have survived the fix intended to cure it. **The disease diagnosed itself on me: I did not verify before filing.** ⇒ **THE REAL DEFECT (proven, measured on the box — see `Langston Design Asks/GOVERNANCE_CHECKER_STALE_LEDGER_ROOT_CAUSE.md`, commit `484a0428b`): the checker reads a FROZEN copy of its own rulebook.** `poller.mjs:313` `loadExceptions()` does `readFileSync(join(REPO_ROOT,'1-system-manual','GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS.md'))` — the **working tree** — while commits (`git log BRANCH`) and `docPresent` grade at **`GOV_REF` (live)**. `git fetch` updates refs, never the working tree; nothing pulls that clone but a redeploy. Measured: `/opt/governance-checker/DawnTraderV3` HEAD `97b56f56c`, ledger mtime **Jun 26 21:51**, **0** P19-B8.4c rows, `GOV_SHADOW=0` (**enforcing**). **Drift is a PROCESS, not a state — measured 336 → 338 → 339 commits behind within a single conversation; it falls further behind on every push and nothing observes it.** **★ COUNT CORRECTED ON-RECORD (CC-B error, caught by Langston before it reached Kyle): CC-B first reported "sees 8 na-skip rows vs 11 at origin." THAT WAS MEASURED ON THE WRONG MACHINE** — the `8` came from `/home/deploy/dawntrader` (the staging **app** clone, irrelevant to the checker), not the checker's `REPO_ROOT`. CC-B corrected the path for the B8.4c row check but carried the stale `8` forward into this entry, the root-cause doc, and three Discord messages. **A number measured on the wrong machine, propagated into a document whose thesis is that a system was reading the wrong machine.** **CANONICAL METHOD (the parser's own rule at `poller.mjs:315-321`, not a preference): split on `|`, require `cells.length >= 7`, `type === 'na-skip'`, `confirmedBy !== 'pending'`. Raw `grep -c` counts prose/legend/header lines; the parser does not.** **MEASURED WITH THAT RULE: the checker honors `na-skip` rows = 1. Origin = 9.** (The three misleading greps: deploy clone `8` = wrong box; checker clone `3` = right box, raw string; checker clone **parsed `1`** = what enforcement actually admits.) **So the checker is not honoring 8-of-11 — it is honoring ONE.** Every other Langston-confirmed N/A on the branch is invisible to it. **The corrected figure strengthens the finding rather than softening it.** **⇒ EVERY exceptions row filed since 2026-06-26 has been INVISIBLE to enforcement.** Auto-resolve ran flawlessly against an empty rulebook. `checker.mjs:28` asserts *"never a stale copy"* — `loadExceptions` is the one place that violates the invariant the file declares. **This single defect explains: `c4c98925` re-surfacing; the recurring "change-class undeclared → strictest" alerts on B8.4b/B8.4c despite declared classes AND filed class-override rows; B8.4b's four doc-gap alerts; and every invisible `open` declaration.** **★ RECURRENCE (OLD Claude): B-GOV-4 already found this same clone stale (at Jun-19) and "fixed" it by deploying — with NOTHING to prevent recurrence. It recurred within two weeks.** A one-time `git pull` is a patch (§8 #11 NO-PATCHES; Langston concurs). **FIX = eliminate the second source of truth, do not synchronize it:** read the ledger at `GOV_REF` (`git show ${BRANCH}:1-system-manual/GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS.md`), and **fail-loud** if that read fails — today an unreadable/absent ledger returns `{}`, i.e. *no suppression at all*, a silent default. **An enforcer that cannot read its rulebook must STOP, not proceed.** Generalized invariant to adopt + CI-enforce (a lint forbidding `readFileSync(REPO_ROOT,…)` for any decision input): **every artifact a decision depends on is read at exactly one ref — the ref being graded.** Then staleness is impossible *by construction*, not by discipline. **★ SEQUENCING (CC-B, load-bearing): this fix is a PREREQUISITE, not a parallel workstream.** Shipping a stricter predicate (e.g. home-must-be-an-OPEN-batch) onto an enforcer with a frozen ledger makes it hard-fail correct homes and pass dead ones, authoritatively — *"not a hardening; an outage with a compliance badge."* **Original (superseded) leg-(a) text, kept for the record:** ~~when a doc-gap alert's disposition is recorded as a Langston-confirmed `na-skip` / `class-override` row, the checker should treat that ledger entry as AUTO-RESOLVING the alert; today it doesn't, so every legitimately-N/A doc gap needs a separate manual `resolve` CLI call, and a missed call = the alert re-surfaces on the widening back-off and escalates to Kyle~~ (exactly what happened: B8.4c's N/A row landed 2026-07-08, correctly, but the alert was only `ack`'d — never `resolve`'d — and re-surfaced twice over ~36h, pulling Langston + Kyle into triage for already-completed work; Langston resolved it manually 2026-07-10). **(b) no acked-but-unresolved sweep:** the §10.5 per-turn check as practiced greps `state=active AND acknowledged_at=null`, which STRUCTURALLY CANNOT SEE an alert sitting in `acknowledged` — so "queue clean" can be reported while owned-but-unclosed alerts accumulate. Live evidence (found 2026-07-10): **four alerts stuck in `acknowledged` since June** — `6f8db90b` (B-NEW-53.1 live re-confirm, 06-08), `c2aa2940` (B-NEW-53.2 live confirm, 06-08), `da0c24b8` (B-4.7 R2 closure, 06-12, acked by langston), `06532d55` (B-4.6-B formal 24h gate read, 06-13). Each is a verification claimed-but-never-closed; each needs an owner disposition (was the verification actually performed → `resolve`, or is it real open work?). **FIX:** (a) checker treats a Langston-confirmed exceptions row as auto-resolve for that doc key; (b) add an acked-but-unresolved sweep to the §10.5 check (both CLAUDE.md §10.5 + the MEMORY session-start step, which today only look at active+unacked) and/or a dispatcher escalation for `acknowledged` past TTL. **HOME (§9.4, CC-B + Langston consensus 2026-07-10): NEW batch `B-ALERT-LIFECYCLE`** — a single checker-hardening batch owning alert-lifecycle correctness (Langston: "they're one subsystem … bundle into a single checker-hardening batch, not two"). **★ HOME CORRECTED same-day: this was first homed to `B-GOV-4`, which is WRONG — B-GOV-4 was EXECUTED + CLOSED 2026-06-26 (GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS `open-retired` row; it shipped the #350 `extractBatchId` fix). Homing an open issue to a CLOSED batch is a dead home — precisely the "named home that never happens" failure §9.4 exists to prevent. Caught by CC-B before dispatching the batch-id to Langston.** Scope of `B-ALERT-LIFECYCLE` — **★ FINAL AFTER HAND-OFF (Langston ruling + CC-B co-sign, 2026-07-10): ONE leg.** Leg (a) **auto-resolve** stays here. **Leg (b) — the acked-but-unresolved DETECTOR — is HANDED OFF to `B-ALERT-TAXONOMY` OBJ-6** (with OBJ-6b, the mint-time observability check): the detector is a taxonomy/alert-state concern, and duplicating it across two batches is worse than the seam. `B-ALERT-LIFECYCLE` **CONSUMES** that detector. **⚠️ BINDING CONDITION (Langston): the detector's output MUST be a queryable STATE/EVENT on the alert (a re-surface record), NOT a Discord re-post side-effect** — else auto-resolve has nothing to hook. Contract defined in the OBJ-6 scope before build. **⚠️ `#443` IS THEREFORE BLOCKED-ON `B-ALERT-TAXONOMY` OBJ-6** (CC-B condition, written down so the seam cannot rot): without the dependency recorded, someone picks up #443, finds auto-resolve has nothing to consume, and it stalls or gets "closed as unbuildable" — **a dependency that isn't written down is a dead home with extra steps.** **⚠️ EVIDENCE GUARD:** the stale-ack detector must **SURFACE** `6f8db90b` / `c2aa2940` / `06532d55` (they are genuinely neglected — that is the point) but must **NEVER auto-resolve or "tidy" them**; they stay `acknowledged` until `B-VERIFY-BACKLOG` runs the queries, because **they are the evidence for #445.** **Clock (Langston, sharper than CC-B's proposal):** key the stale-ack clock off **`acknowledged_at`** — measure neglect from the moment the alert was SILENCED, not from `triggers_at` — and make N **per-class configurable**: a soak verification legitimately sits 21d, a reminder must not sit 3. **★ SCOPE CORRECTION ON-RECORD (2026-07-10): a home-validation leg was briefly added as leg (c) and is now REMOVED to its own issue #444.** Langston's two stateless review passes landed opposite ("make it leg (c)" then "make it its own issue"); CC-B reconciled to the LATTER because its reasoning is stronger and is corroborated by his own earlier guardrail: home-validation operates on a DIFFERENT artifact (parse the home string out of RUNNING_ISSUES/roadmap, cross-check batch open/closed state in BATCH_CATALOG / GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS) — different input, different parser, different failure — so bolting it onto a clean two-leg queue-checker blurs what this batch is. Recorded rather than silently rewritten. **Plus a PRE-REQ: disposition the four stuck June alerts with citations** — per Langston, each traced from the paper trail: verification demonstrably ran (completion report / log / DB evidence) → `resolve` WITH that evidence in the note; no evidence → it is REAL OPEN WORK, own it and give **it** its own home. **Four dispositions with citations, NOT four resolves.** Langston takes Step-4 on the diff. ↔ #350, `ALERT_HANDLING_PROTOCOL.md`. **OPEN (homed: B-ALERT-LIFECYCLE).**
- **#445 OPEN 2026-07-10 (CC-B trace + Langston queue-forensics + OLD Claude; the finding behind #443) — FOUR batches were CLOSED on the promise of a live/at-scale verification that was NEVER PERFORMED, and the governance status fields still read "alert-gated" to this day.** This is **ONE process gap that produced four instances**, not four independent data gaps (Langston). The pattern: a batch could not verify at deploy time (sparse cadence / regime not yet observed / 24h window not elapsed), so it CLOSED by **scheduling a system-alert to do the check later** — then nobody ever wrote the result back. The alert got `ack`'d and rotted in `acknowledged` (→ #443 leg (b), which is the only mechanism that would have caught this). **Full citation table: `Claude Comms and Packages/Langston Design Asks/B_ALERT_LIFECYCLE_FOUR_ALERT_DISPOSITION.md`.** Summary — **NONE earns a `resolve`:** (1) **`6f8db90b`** B-NEW-53.1 crypto admitted 13 fields "still 100% AT SCALE" → **NO EVIDENCE**; only **n=1** row (ESPORTS/USD) ever verified (`BATCH_B_NEW_53_1_COMPLETION_REPORT.md:10`); status still "alert-gated" (`RUNNING_ISSUES.md:684`, `BATCH_CATALOG.md:318`). **★ Supersession hypothesis REJECTED (Langston):** the later parity re-run (`7362f63f`) measured **provenance coverage** (100% of 1.64M base decisions carry a provenance record) — a **DIFFERENT PROPERTY** from "the 13 admission fields are each populated at 100% across admitted crypto rows at scale." Provenance-present ≠ fields-populated. No such query exists → open work. (2) **`c2aa2940`** B-NEW-53.2 xStock at-entry block "live confirm" → **NO EVIDENCE**; confirmation is code-review + deploy-boot only; the report's own "**if green** → CLOSED; **if still blank** → reopen #208" branch was never taken (`BATCH_B_NEW_53_2_COMPLETION_REPORT.md:10,37`). **★ Queue forensics (Langston, decisive): fired `16:05:04.792Z` → acked `16:05:20.078Z` = ~15 SECONDS. No live xStock at-entry-block check happens in fifteen seconds — the ack was reflexive, not a verification.** (3) **`da0c24b8`** B-4.7 R2 first fresh TFS/IE `strong_bull_trend` admit → **NO EVIDENCE**; both classes sat in `STRUCTURAL_TRANSITION` all post-deploy so the path was never observed exercising (`B_4_7_COMPLETION_REPORT.md:32`); **OLD Claude disposed it correctly + refused to resolve** — `strong_bull_trend` appears 67,021× in log tails but a **token count is not an evaluation event** (the string appears in every regime→strategy mapping diagnostic). (4) **`06532d55`** B-4.6-B **formal 24h gate read on the AMENDED gate Kyle personally accepted** → **PARTIAL, the worst of the four**: the ONLY trace it ran is a prose Telegram line ("the 24-hour check … passed cleanly on every measure"); **no numbers, no comparison against the pinned residual ceiling, no closure entry**; `#225` still OPEN carrying only the ~6h/11.5h samples; the completion report **still says** "Status: COMPLETE pending the formal 24h gate read" (`B_4_6B_CHUNK_B_COMPLETION_REPORT.md:3`). **Per rule-13 that prose claim is not decision-grade — it is not even a snapshot, it is an assertion.** **★ TWO FAILURE CLASSES — with a CORRECTION ON-RECORD (do not silently rewrite):** **(I) UN-RUN** — the verification was performable but nobody performed it. **(II) UN-RUNNABLE** — a verification scheduled against a condition that **structurally could not occur**, i.e. doomed at creation; it would sit *legitimately* in `acknowledged` forever, so **no stale-ack sweep (#443 leg (b)) can ever catch it.** **★ CORRECTION (2026-07-10, CC-B; caught by Langston, settled by OLD Claude's code-check): `da0c24b8` was FIRST filed here as the Class (II) instance on the rationale that "the `strong_bull_trend` admit branch cannot fire with active trading OFF since Phase 8." THAT RATIONALE WAS WRONG — CC-B asserted a mechanism the cited evidence did not support** (`B_4_7_COMPLETION_REPORT.md:32` gives a *regime-occurrence* blocker, not an active-trading gate). **OLD Claude's code-check settled it with structured rows, not a log grep:** `vts-runner.ts:1282` has `case 'strong_bull_trend'`; `:1598` "Phase 14: Governance gate REMOVED from VTS path — generates trades for ALL strategies"; `:2425` carries `gate_admitted = (regimeLabel === TREND_FRIENDLY_STABLE)`; `:2504` writes `rejectStage:'admitted'`. **`signal_eval_archive` shows 1,002 ADMITTED `strong_bull_trend` rows post-cutoff (749 TFS + 253 IE), first admit 2026-06-11T06:40:39Z — with active trading OFF.** The admit branch is a **regime-occurrence gate, not an active-execution gate**. **⇒ `da0c24b8` is Class (I), and its R2 condition is SATISFIED ON REAL EVIDENCE** (TFS first_seen 2026-06-11T01:03:09Z / 7,654,453 rows; IE 01:03:14Z / 1,614,295 rows; 491,009 `strong_bull_trend` evaluations under TFS/IE, 1,002 admitted) → **`RESOLVED` by cc-a with that evidence attached** — the only one of the four that earns a resolve, and it earns it on data, not on a token count (OLD Claude refused an earlier 67k-grep "proof": *"a token count is not an evaluation event"*). **⇒ CLASS (II) THEREFORE HAS ZERO CONFIRMED INSTANCES.** It remains a real hazard class (the mint-time hole is genuine, and no sweep can catch it), but it is a **preventive guard against a hazard we have not actually observed** — not a fix for an active defect. Stated plainly so the ledger does not imply otherwise. **★★ FALSE-PREMISE FINDING (OLD Claude, and it escalates this entry): `B_4_7_COMPLETION_REPORT.md:32` justified leaving R2 open by asserting "both classes sat in STRUCTURAL_TRANSITION through the post-chunk-B window." THE ARCHIVE FLATLY CONTRADICTS THAT — TFS appeared within NINE SECONDS of the cutoff and then dominated with 7.65M rows.** So the verification was **schedulable and answerable the entire time**, and a CLOSED batch's completion report carries a **factually false justification** for deferring it. This is worse than "un-run": it is **un-run on a false premise recorded in the governance record**. A completion report is supposed to be the authoritative account; here it asserted a market condition that the data disproves. **HOMES (§13, Langston's rulings — "reopening #208 is not a home, it's a loop"):** • **Class (I) UN-RUN, still open — `6f8db90b`, `c2aa2940`, `06532d55` → ONE dated re-verification batch `B-VERIFY-BACKLOG`** which (i) re-runs each at-scale query **live against staging**, (ii) **writes the result back**, and (iii) enforces that each status field flips from "alert-gated" to **"confirmed (query, n, date)"** or **"reopened"** — **never left as-is**. All three are observable TODAY (VTS admits crypto + xStock rows continuously; a timing read needs only a 24h window). • **`da0c24b8` → RESOLVED (evidence above), NOT re-homed to B8.5.** • **Class (II) mint-time observability guard** (a scheduled verification must be checked for observability-under-current-config at creation, and re-checked on config change) → folded into the alert-taxonomy/detector work; explicitly PREVENTIVE, zero known instances, and must NOT be prioritized over the three genuinely-broken verifications above. Together these close the PROCESS gap, not just the instances. **★ CANDIDATE STANDING RULE for Kyle (not adopted unilaterally): a batch may NOT close on an alert-gated verification unless the result is written back into the governance record; "scheduled a check" is not "verified."** ↔ #443 (leg (b) is the detector), #207, #208, #217, #225, B-NEW-53.3. **OPEN (homed: B-VERIFY-BACKLOG).**

Pin WHERE the evidence lives before hashing, or the integrity check is itself a "trust me."** ↔ #443, #445, #446, #448, #449. **HOME (§9.4): `B-GOV-INTEGRITY-1` (owner CC-A, due 2026-07-12), which absorbs `B-ALERT-TAXONOMY`; blocked on `B-GOV-INTEGRITY-0`. - **#448 OPEN 2026-07-10 (CC-A + Langston Step-2 Findings B/C, both independently verified in code by CC-A) — there are THREE uncoordinated definitions of the alert `category` vocabulary, and the HTTP endpoint's allowlist omits `governance` — so 181 of 254 alerts (71% of the ledger) cannot be filtered in the UI at all.** `server/routes.ts:6628` hard-codes `const CATEGORIES = new Set(['soak_verification','health_check','breakage','one_off','recurring'])` and silently narrows an unrecognized `?category=` to `undefined` (no filter). **`governance` — the single largest category, by a factor of six — is not in it**, nor is `verification` (5 rows), nor the proposed `report`. **The three drifting definitions:** (1) the engine union `AlertCategory` (`server/services/system-alerts.ts:~46`); (2) this route allowlist; (3) the CLI's blind cast (`scripts/system-alerts.ts:169` + `:230`, `requireFlag(args,'category') as AlertCategory`) — **a literal TYPE ASSERTION, telling the compiler "trust me" instead of checking. That cast is HOW the 9 untyped strings drifted into the data.** The root defect of #446 is, at the source level, **an assertion standing in for a check** — the same shape as #447 and #449. **FIX (NO-PATCHES, rule 15 — do NOT ship the taxonomy as a patch over three drifting definitions):** collapse to **ONE exported SSOT constant** imported by engine + route + CLI, and put the validation gate in **`addAlert` (the library boundary)**, not only in CLI arg-parse — otherwise the next programmatic caller re-opens #446. The route allowlist then derives from the SSOT and can never drift again. **★ Migration hazard (Langston, with a substrate correction from CC-A):** Langston wrote *"the migration must be raw SQL `UPDATE` bypassing `addAlert`"* — **the alerts are NOT in Postgres; they are a JSONL file.** His hazard is real, the mechanism differs: the migration is a **whole-file rewrite that never calls `addAlert` at all**, so it structurally cannot pass the new validation boundary (good) — but it has **no validator, no transaction, and no atomicity**, and a crash mid-write truncates the ledger. **Required:** write temp → fsync → **atomic `rename()`**; record the pre-mutation `sha256` of the `.bak` **in the migration commit** (*a baseline nothing proves is not a baseline*); and **F5 validation binds NEW MINTS ONLY**, because six of the migrated values are by definition outside the new closed union — a validator applied to historical rows makes the migration unable to represent its own pre-state. **★ Verify content-conservation, NOT row-count (Langston): "254 → 254" is a count invariant impersonating an immutability invariant.** Hash `(id, state, acknowledged_at, acknowledged_by)` — **fields that EXIST pre-F3b, see #447** — across all rows OUTSIDE the intended-changed set, before == after; plus CC-B's four-row ASSERT (`6f8db90b`/`c2aa2940`/`06532d55` stay `acknowledged`; `da0c24b8` stays `resolved`) protecting #445's evidence. **The migration mutates `category` and nothing else. `test` row `beffed6d` is BACKFILLED to `one_off`+resolved, NOT deleted — 254 stays 254** (Langston's stricter of two contradictory rulings, taken per #401; *deleting a ledger row to tidy a count is a miniature of the disease*). **13 programmatic `addAlert` callers exist** (cron-fire-evidence-verifier, amr-input-health, amr-weather-report, active-engine-service, equity-spot-archiver, daily-loss-budget, portfolio-anchor-service, database-monitor, index.ts, xstock active-dispatch, b-storage-archival-health, b75-cold-liveness, b75-retention-sweep) — all pass only `breakage`/`health_check`/`one_off`, so **no call-site edits are needed PROVIDED those three stay canonical** (Langston Finding D — state the dependency; don't hide it behind "zero code references the legacy strings"). **Pinned closed union (7): `breakage`, `health_check` → 🚨 system · `governance` → 📋 governance · `one_off`, `recurring`, `verification` → ⏰ reminder · `report` → 📊 report (NEW). `soak_verification` (26 rows) FOLDS INTO `verification` and leaves the union** (Langston Finding A — otherwise the migration re-introduces the very overlap it exists to kill). **Out of scope, named so nobody conflates the namespaces (Finding E):** `server/services/alerts-service.ts` + `server/jobs/feed-integrity-auto-check.ts` are a SEPARATE DB-backed user-facing alert system with its own `category` union (`informational|actionable|critical`). ↔ #446 (absorbed), #447, #449. **HOME (§9.4): `B-GOV-INTEGRITY-1` (the F5 leg; owner CC-A, due 2026-07-12), blocked on `B-GOV-INTEGRITY-0`.** **OPEN (homed).**
- **#446 OPEN 2026-07-10 (CC-A, B-ALERT-TAXONOMY Step-1 live-queue probe) — ⚠️ CORRECTED ON-RECORD 2026-07-10, NOT silently rewritten: this entry's blast-radius claim below — *"`category` is render + a single `listAlerts` query filter"* — was ITSELF AN UNVERIFIED ASSERTION and is NOW KNOWN FALSE. `server/routes.ts:6628` is a SECOND consumer carrying a THIRD, drifted definition of the set (see #448). I wrote a blast-radius "gate PASSED + proven" on a grep I had not exhausted, in the pre-audit for a batch about assertions replacing measurements. The batch is re-homed to `B-GOV-INTEGRITY-1` (blocked on `B-GOV-INTEGRITY-0`); the taxonomy is demoted from headline to one objective of a governance-integrity program, because relabeling the alerts does NOT fix Kyle's problem and shipping it as though it did would be the disease performed on its own cure. Original text follows unaltered.** — the §10.5 alert `category` field is UNCONTROLLED FREE TEXT, so every alert renders as `🚨 SYSTEM ALERT` and any category→class mapping silently erodes.** Kyle's directive (2026-07-10): *"a system alert should be specifically for errors… a governance issue should be labelled something else, and the alarms that we set so we don't forget… something else."* **Probe of the live 254-row queue: 13 distinct categories in use; only 6 are in the `AlertCategory` union; 9 are UNTYPED** (`verification`, `scheduled_verification`, `weekend_restart_verification`, `b46b_soak_analysis`, `reorg_b2_1_window`, `tec_selfheal_verify`, `comms_decommission`, `reminder`, `test`) — while `health_check` is **typed but never used** (0 rows). A perfect inversion: what's used isn't typed; what's typed isn't used. **Root cause:** the CLI (`system-alerts -- add --category <anything>`) accepts arbitrary strings, so every batch minted its own. **Two consequences:** (a) `alertClassOf(category)` can only be NO-PATCHES-clean if the set is CLOSED *and* closure is enforced at the write boundary — otherwise the next invented string falls through to the default, which today renders as SYSTEM ALERT (**exactly Kyle's complaint**); (b) typed callers cannot compile against categories the CLI writes (hit building the freshness monitor: `'verification'` is not assignable to `AlertCategory`). **★ Quantified: 181/253 alerts (72%) are `governance`** — three-quarters of what shouts SYSTEM ALERT at Kyle is the governance checker. **A SECOND defect found in the same probe: `info`-severity alerts NEVER post to Discord** (the webhook is gated to warning+critical), and scheduled reminders are typically `info` — so **the alarms we set precisely so we don't forget are the class least likely to reach Kyle** (e.g. the Aug-1 Wave-D forward-coverage verification, `verification`/`info`, would have fired into the queue and never surfaced). **FIX (Langston-ruled, two gates — close BOTH or it returns): (1) ADD-TIME — the CLI REJECTS an unknown `--category` and echoes the valid set (fail loud at mint time; this is what makes the set genuinely closed rather than closed-by-convention); (2) RENDER-TIME — `alertClassOf()` on an unknown category THROWS/logs loud and NEVER defaults to SYSTEM ALERT (the silent default IS the bug).** Plus: posting becomes **class-driven, not severity-driven** (`reminder`+`report` post at any severity; `system`+`governance` keep the warning/critical gate), a NEW `report` category (a display class over `health_check` structurally could not separate the weekly report from a feed-stall watchdog — both are `health_check`), and a one-shot **MUTATE** backfill of the 9 legacy strings to canonical (blast-radius gate PASSED + proven: `category` is render + a single `listAlerts` query filter; zero code references the legacy strings; the Langston bridge does NOT route on it; no analytics/ML/join consumer; not persisted-and-read like the `paper_sim` discriminator #405 ⇒ mutate, don't alias, per rule 18). **Outbound-behavior delta to state explicitly at close: info-severity alerts will now reach Discord that never did before.** **HOME: batch `B-ALERT-TAXONOMY` OBJ-1 (in flight; Step-1 APPROVED-WITH-CHANGES).** **OPEN (homed).**
- **#444 OPEN 2026-07-10 (CC-B, split out of #443 per Langston's later ruling) — a §9.4 "named home" is never validated to be an OPEN batch, so an issue homed onto a CLOSED batch grades green and silently dies.** §9.4 requires every surfaced issue to get a concrete named home (a batch id / roadmap item / dated task), and the governance-checker grades a batch's doc-set — but **nothing anywhere checks that a cited home batch-id resolves to a batch that is still open.** An issue homed to a shipped batch therefore reads as correctly-homed while its work can never be picked up: it is §9.4's own failure mode wearing a compliance badge (Langston). **★ TWO CONFIRMED INSTANCES, HOURS APART, BY DIFFERENT PARTIES — the class is NOT theoretical.** **(1)** #443 was first homed to `B-GOV-4`, EXECUTED + CLOSED 2026-06-26 (its `open-retired` row is in GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS; it shipped the #350 `extractBatchId` fix). CC-B caught it only by manually verifying the id before dispatching to Langston — nothing systemic would have caught it. **(2) SAME DAY, in a ruling ABOUT closure integrity, Langston homed "closure-integrity" to `B-GOV-4` again.** CC-B objected with the citations (`BATCH_CATALOG.md:387` — B-GOV-4 shipped, CI `28266998733` green, deployed at HEAD `97b56f56c`, full doc-set; `GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS.md:28` `open-retired` discharge); **Langston verified and confirmed the objection holds.** So within a single day the same dead batch was cited as a live home twice, by two different parties, one of whom was reviewing the very issue that documents the failure. **Neither of us has a mechanism that catches it — that IS this issue.** Instance (2) is the stronger evidence: a reviewer applying full attention, in-context, still did it. **FIX:** the governance-checker validates that a cited HOME batch-id resolves to an OPEN batch, and FLAGS it when it does not. **Distinct from #443's legs by construction (Langston's ruling for the split):** #443 (a)/(b) operate on the ALERTS-QUEUE lifecycle (`system-alerts` state); this operates on a DIFFERENT artifact — it must parse the home string out of `RUNNING_ISSUES.md` / roadmap items and cross-check batch open/closed state in `BATCH_CATALOG.md` and/or the GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS `open-retired` rows. Different input, different parser, different failure. **Known hard part (Langston's guardrail, inherited):** batch-state resolution may be non-trivial — *retired* vs *closed* vs *superseded* are more than one terminal state, and the home string is free text. Scope conservatively; certainty-before-cutting applies to scope too. **HOME (§9.4): `B-HOME-VALIDATION`, folding into `B-GOV-INTEGRITY-2` as F6/F6b — OWNER: CC-B · DUE: 2026-07-13.** Sequenced AFTER the checker-currency work (both touch the checker; serialize to avoid a two-session collision on the same tooling). **★ CONCEDED ON-RECORD (OLD Claude's catch, and it is the sharpest question anyone asked): as first filed, this issue's home had NO OWNER and NO DUE DATE — meaning `#444` FAILED THE VERY PREDICATE (F6b) IT PROPOSES.** An issue demanding that homes be validated was given a home its own validator would reject. **That is not irony, it is the evidence:** the predicate is necessary precisely because the person who invented it could not comply with it unaided. Owner + due date assigned 2026-07-10; it stops failing its own gate today. **★ Langston's ruling: make `#444` the FIRST ACCEPTANCE TEST of F6 — dogfood it.** F6 passes iff its predicate rejects the exact commit in which this issue was homed to the closed `B-GOV-4`. ↔ #443, #350, #449, §9.4. **OPEN (homed: B-GOV-INTEGRITY-2 / F6+F6b · owner CC-B · due 2026-07-13).**

- **#419 OPEN 2026-07-08 (Langston B8.4b Step-4 minor, homed by CC-B) — active-funnel RTB-refresh sub-stage won't balance under error rows.** In `ready_to_buy_service.refreshAndRank`, the per-signal path ticks `refreshedAttempted` as a signal enters re-eval, then records `reconfirmed` (survived re-SQE) or `rejectedInRefresh` (failed re-SQE). But the `catch` block bulk-deletes the row on a processing error WITHOUT ticking either outcome — so under errors `refreshedAttempted > reconfirmed + rejectedInRefresh` and the refresh sub-stage doesn't add up on the panel. Honest gap, not a double-count; DORMANT until B8.5 (active path off, no refresh cycles run). **Fix:** add an `error` outcome bucket to `RtbRefreshCounters` (+ envelope + client row) and tick it in the catch, so the sub-stage balances. **HOME (§13): B8.5 (paper-active switch-on) — folds into the B8.5 test-harness/observability pass alongside #393/#391; the funnel first shows real refresh data at switch-on, so the balance fix lands before any real error row is displayed.** Surfaced by Langston, homed by CC-B. **OPEN (dormant-safe).**

- **#349 — TEC config staleness fence latches-until-restart + freezes the whole VTS cycle (B-NEW-40 soak finding, CC-A 2026-06-25). ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-25 by batch B-TEC-SELFHEAL** (Kyle-greenlit; full 11-step workflow, Langston Step-1/2/4 APPROVED, CI 4-green run 28181014927, deployed staging restart#417 HTTP 200, all 4 TEC classes fresh + zero TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED post-deploy). **Fix shipped:** OBJ-1 = the lazy-refresh trigger relocated to a single-call-site helper at the TOP of `resolveTECConfig` (before both throws) so a stale-past-ceiling consult schedules its own coalesced refresh and self-heals in ~1 cycle (was: latch-until-restart); OBJ-2 = per-trade try/catch in the VTS exit loop (mirror `paper-execution-engine.ts:794`) so one stale-class trade no longer aborts the whole cycle. Fail-closed fence byte-identical (the safety property is preserved — OBJ-5(c)). **Unprimed-class delta (Langston Step-4 note):** the new `!tecConfigCache.has` guard ALSO removed a prior incidental behavior — an unprimed class no longer schedules a (pointless) refresh before `TEC_CACHE_MISS_FATAL`; correct, since boot hard-fail means a live process never has an unprimed class. **OBJ-5 real-world close:** deterministic self-heal proven by the 48-test TEC suite; the latch-vs-self-heal real-world proof is the 06-28 xStock weekend reopen — scheduled §10.5 verification alert (`tec_selfheal_verify`, fires 06-29 06:00 UTC) confirms it self-heals vs the old 17h cascade. **OBJ-3 considered-and-declined stands (below).** _(Diagnosis history retained below.)_ ★ **DEEPER ROOT (confirmed CC-A + Langston 2026-06-25, supersedes the cold-cache-at-reopen framing below):** the reopen staleness is one TRIGGER of a general defect — the fail-closed fence is CORRECT and stays, but (1) the staleness THROW (`resolveTECConfig` ~:248) is BEFORE the lazy refresh trigger (~:271) so the cache can never self-heal — it latches until a process restart (no periodic refresh exists); and (2) the VTS exit loop (`vts-runner.ts:2421`) has no per-trade try/catch, so one stale-class open trade aborts the WHOLE multi-class cycle (exit-mgmt FIRST at `:3273` before scan/open at `:3293` → opens blocked too). Live proof: 06-22 reopen stuck ~17h continuous until a deploy restart; crypto_spot also stuck ~5h (not weekend-only). The fix = **B-TEC-SELFHEAL**: OBJ-1 fire the refresh through the coalescer before the throw (self-heal in ~1 cycle); OBJ-2 per-trade exit-loop isolation (mirror the already-correct `paper-execution-engine.ts:794`). The reopen-warm framing (below) is DROPPED — self-heal is general. **★ OBJ-3 (bounded periodic re-warm timer) — CONSIDERED-AND-DECLINED (Langston Step-1, 2026-06-25):** OBJ-1+OBJ-2 are the complete root fix; a timer would only suppress one correct-by-design transient throw for a zero-consumer class, at the cost of an always-on singleton + new failure surface on a mode-shared safety path (gold-plating; NO-PATCHES cuts toward minimal). **Conditional reopen trigger:** B-TEC-SELFHEAL OBJ-5 must observe a zero-consumer class self-healing within ~1 cycle; IF staging ever shows that first-consult blip is MATERIAL on the active/paper path (real open positions), OBJ-3 reopens as a named follow-up. _(Original soak-finding detail retained below for the record.)_ The B-NEW-40 14-day soak verification (alert `b83b1e4b`, run 2026-06-25 against deploy-ts 2026-05-17) returned **FAIL on its presence-check** (3754 raw `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` log lines ≈ ~1877 distinct incidents, clustered on just 3 days: 2026-06-15 / 06-22 / 06-23, then STOPPED — nothing since). **BUT the failure mode B-NEW-40 actually fixed did NOT recur:** pool/socket errors = **0** over 38.9 days (pg-pool keepalive held). The stale events are a DIFFERENT, narrower cause: **`assetClass=xstock_spot`, cache age ~25h, "Refusing to honor a stale kill-switch snapshot,"** every one *contained* inside a VTS cycle (`[ITEM4][VTS] Cycle error (contained)`) — so zero live impact while active trading is OFF. **Root mechanism — CORRECTED 2026-06-25 (Kyle architectural context + CC-A code-confirmation; supersedes the initial "VTS consults during the close" read):** VTS does NOT consult `xstock_spot` during the weekend close — it is correctly PAUSED. xStock runs a weekend shutdown 8PM Fri→8PM Sun ET (`market-hours.ts:79-85` `isInXstockWeekendClose`; poll-reconciled by `session-lifecycle-controller.ts` since the B-NEW-52 cron retirement): at shutdown `runWeekendShutdownCore` calls `xstockSpotScanner.pause()` + suspends open trades; the VTS per-pair eval also returns early on `!isXstockMarketOpenUTC` (`eval-cycle.ts:312-314`) BEFORE any TEC consult. So nothing consults the xStock TEC config all weekend. The TEC config cache is primed ONLY at boot (`primeTECConfig()`, `server/index.ts:815`) and otherwise lazy-on-consult (`trailing-exit-controller.ts:160`). **The staleness is therefore a COLD-CACHE-AT-REOPEN, not a during-close-consult bug:** across the weekend pause the config goes stale (un-consulted), and at Sunday 8PM ET reopen — when `runWeekendRestartCore` resumes the scanner — the FIRST consults fail-closed on the stale config until the lazy refresh lands. **Timing corroborates:** the 06-15 / 06-22 clusters are at ~00:00 UTC = Sun 20:00 ET = the reopen edge (events tick once/min with cache-age growing +60000ms, i.e. one class failing repeatedly until the refresh catches up). The weekend pre-warm (`runWeekendRestartCore:430` `runPrewarmWithCircuitBreaker`) refreshes the OHLC **price** snapshot at reopen but does **NOT** re-prime the TEC **config** snapshot — that is the exact gap. **Live state 2026-06-25 also confirms:** the two TRADED spot classes (`crypto_spot`/`xstock_spot`) are fresh (consulted every ~60s); the two PERP classes (`crypto_perp`/`xstock_perp`) sit stale-by-ceiling (~11.4h, since the last restart) with `consecutiveFailCount=0` — **benign: nothing consults perps (we trade spot only), so they legitimately aren't refreshed; no fence fires for them.** **Why this matters:** harmless now (VTS-contained), but when xStock active-trading turns ON (Phase 19), a stale `xstock_spot` kill-switch snapshot would fail-CLOSE and BLOCK xStock trades during/after every market-closed gap.

**Refresh internals (CC-A read `refreshTECConfigForClass` @ `trailing-exit-controller.ts:361`):** the refresh pulls ONLY DB `module_constants` rows (break_even_enabled, trigger_r, target_lock_r, trail_distance, …) — **pure config, ZERO live-market-data dependency** — so warming it is cheap and has no venue dependency (it works fine even with the market closed). This is what makes the reopen-warm fix below trivial and correct.

**ROOT FORK — RESOLVED 2026-06-25 (Kyle context + CC-A code + Langston/NEW Claude concur):** the earlier open question ("should VTS consult `xstock_spot` during a closed window?") is answered **NO and it doesn't** — VTS is correctly paused for the whole close (scanner pause + `eval-cycle.ts:312-314` early-return). So there is no closed-window consult to gate, no continuous 24/7 keep-warm timer to build, and NEW Claude's reorg owes nothing on this sub-line (Langston confirmed the reorg-homed fork closes clean). The problem is purely the **reopen edge**.

**FIX [Phase-19, scoped + simple]:** extend the EXISTING Sunday-reopen hook — `runWeekendRestartCore` (`session-lifecycle-controller.ts:417-465`), which already runs the OHLC price pre-warm at `:430` — to ALSO re-prime the TEC **config** for the resuming xStock class(es) at reopen (call `primeTECConfig()` / the per-class equivalent, exactly as boot does at `server/index.ts:815`). This is the §11-blessed **cold-start-warmup-at-resumption** pattern Kyle explicitly endorses; it mirrors what the price snapshot already does. **NOT** a new always-on timer, and **NOT** extending the 300000ms ceiling (that would weaken the fail-closed fence — §11-prohibited; the fence is behaving correctly). Tiny additional guard worth a line: warming runs even though the venue is closed, which is fine because the refresh is pure DB config (no live-data dependency, confirmed above). _(This supersedes the earlier "continuous keep-warm timer + derived-interval + self-pruning-membership" design — that machinery is unnecessary once the cause is pinned to the reopen edge rather than a continuous gap.)_
- **(c) soak/diagnostic FAIL-surface fix (stands):** key it on the **harm signal — a `consecutiveFailCount` that SURVIVES a refresh cycle (i.e. `> 1`, not `> 0`)**, NOT raw staleness presence and NOT an asset-class-name exclusion (a name allowlist is the §11-disliked placeholder that goes stale the day we trade perps). **Why `>1` not `>0` (Langston):** a count of exactly 1 is the benign lazy-refresh catch-up race itself — the single post-expiry consult that fail-closes while the async refresh is mid-flight — so `>0` would light up on the HEALTHY path; require it to survive one refresh cycle so only genuine stuck-stale surfaces. Drops the benign unconsulted perps (`consecutiveFailCount=0`), auto-covers perps if they ever go live, surfaces the genuinely-consulted `xstock_spot`; **auto-clears when the count resets to 0** on a successful warm tick (no sticky red on a self-healed transient).
- **(d) active-trading-path check [Phase-19]:** the active path relies on the **same upstream scanner pause** for its weekend gating (no independent market-hours gate confirmed inside `active-dispatch.ts`) — confirm/where-needed add an explicit gate when active trading is turned on. **(e) governance gap — ✅ DONE 2026-06-25 (CC-A):** the weekend pause state (`scanner.isPaused` SSOT + lifecycle `inFlight` mutex — pauses BOTH paths) was missing from the SIM Cross-Cutting Liveness Registry → added as **S18** (mode-invariant control state).
- **#350 — Governance-checker re-grades a CLOSED/grandfathered batch when a later commit merely REFERENCES its batch-id (parser false-positive). OPEN — homed to B-GOV-4 (near-term governance-tooling batch; Langston-agreed §13 home, 2026-06-25).** Trigger found live: CC-A's 2026-06-25 soak-verification follow-up commits LED their subject with `B-NEW-40` (e.g. `B-NEW-40 soak finding: RUNNING_ISSUES #349 …`). `extractBatchId(commit.subject)` parsed `B-NEW-40` as the commit's OWN batch, giving the long-closed B-NEW-40 a post-`GOV_CUTOFF` `lastCode` → un-grandfathered it → graded it → fired two false alerts (`90ace091` undeclared-class→arch-default, `8dbd9524` missing system_manual). Resolved for THIS instance via a `langston`-confirmed `non_architecture` + `system_manual` N/A in `GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS.md`, but the PARSER will keep doing this whenever any commit references an old batch-id for context. **★ ESCALATION 2026-06-25 — the parser matches batch-ids ANYWHERE in a subject, not just leading, AND it false-fires for NOT-YET-EXISTENT batches too.** Confirmed live: `B-GOV-4` (a future, un-executed batch referenced only MID-subject in two #350-documenting commits — "…concretize #350 B-GOV-4 home", "…parser-fix at #350 → B-GOV-4") was graded → fired **8** false alerts (undeclared-class + 7 missing-doc: scope/pre_audit/completion_report/batch_catalog/phase_history/system_manual/sim). Ironically the commits documenting this bug triggered it. Two consequences: (i) the false-positive isn't limited to closed/grandfathered batches — a batch-id that has NEVER existed gets graded as a closed-batch-needing-docs; (ii) my CLAUDE.md §2 "don't LEAD with a closed id" convention is INSUFFICIENT (mid-subject references trigger it too). Cleared B-GOV-4 via an `open` declaration in GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS.md + 8 alerts resolved. **This makes B-GOV-4 more urgent — it actively floods 8 alerts per stray reference.** **★ SECOND FAILURE MODE 2026-06-25 (multi-hyphen-name TRUNCATION):** the parser also TRUNCATES a multi-segment `B-<NAME>` id — `B-TEC-SELFHEAL` commit subjects were graded as the fragment `B-TEC` (a phantom that has no docs), firing 7 false alerts even though B-TEC-SELFHEAL's full doc-set exists + is closed. So the `B-<NAME>` regex must capture the WHOLE hyphenated name (`B-TEC-SELFHEAL`, not stop at `B-TEC`). Cleared via a `B-TEC` phantom-suppression row in GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS + 7 alerts resolved (Langston gov-triage + CC-A). B-GOV-4 scope now covers BOTH the mid-subject-match AND the multi-hyphen-truncation defects. **Fix (B-GOV-4), now broader:** the checker must only (re)grade a batch-id that is genuinely a batch being WORKED — i.e. (a) appears as the LEADING token of its commit subject (the declared own-batch position) AND (b) is either genuinely NEW (no prior completion report) or carries an explicit re-open signal (new scope file); a batch-id appearing only as a mid-subject contextual reference, or one with no scope/no history at all, must NOT be graded. Pure parser/grading-eligibility change, `change-class: non_architecture` (governance-tooling). **Companion behavior-fix (adopted now, but only a partial mitigation given mid-subject matching):** prefer NOT to put a closed/not-real batch-id token anywhere in a follow-up commit subject (use `#NNN` issue refs or plain descriptors); never LEAD with one. **HOME (concrete — Langston-checked 2026-06-25): B-GOV-4 = the next sequential batch in the established B-GOV governance-tooling family (B-GOV → B-GOV-2 → B-GOV-3 → B-GOV-4), and THIS #350 entry is its scope-seed** (the live tracked home is RUNNING_ISSUES #350 itself — not a floating placeholder; B-GOV-4 is the batch-name that will execute it through the normal workflow). change-class `non_architecture`. **Interim mitigation until it lands:** the commit-naming convention now in CLAUDE.md §2 (don't lead a follow-up subject with a closed batch-id). **✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-26 by B-GOV-4** (CC-A; Langston Step-1/2/4 APPROVED). Shipped, all in `scripts/governance-checker/`: OBJ-1 leading-token grading (`extractLeadingBatchId` — a mid-subject reference no longer establishes a batch), OBJ-2 full multi-hyphen capture (`B-TEC-SELFHEAL` no longer truncated to `B-TEC`), OBJ-3 pin a closed batch's enforcement to its completion-report FIRST-ADD commit time (a later leading re-mention can't un-grandfather it; a post-close scope rev re-enrolls via `Math.max` scope add), OBJ-4 doc-set graded on the completion-report SENTINEL not first-governance-commit (+ order-independent auto-resolve + orphan re-verify at GOV_REF). Proof: 64 poller-logic tests + backtest OBJ-11 GATE PASS + a live behavioral probe vs origin (B-TEC phantom gone, B-TEC-SELFHEAL whole, B-GOV-4 mid-subject not graded, B-NEW-40 pinned+grandfathered). The two `GOVERNANCE_EXCEPTIONS` suppression rows (B-GOV-4 open, B-TEC phantom) are retired post-deploy of the fixed checker. (CI run + deploy id in the B-GOV-4 completion report.)

The widening back-off prevents *recurrence*, but a one-time first-run is unramped. **Fix options (non-blocking): a gentle first-run ramp (cap re-surfaces/tick on the first N ticks), or a one-time backlog triage before activating.** **Dispositive note (proves this is NOT a resolved-re-promotion bug — Langston pushed for the positive proof):** the five alerts cited as "re-surfaced then resolved" (4× weekend_shutdown + EUROP `4cd4975c`) each show `resurface_count=1` (frozen), `last_resurfaced_at=2026-06-23T21:59:28Z` (the e2e fire-due tick, BEFORE the ~22:01 resolves), and the `system-alerts-dispatcher.timer` demonstrably fired AGAIN at 22:47:40Z (post-resolve, on its 15-min cadence) WITHOUT incrementing the count — so a dispatcher tick ran after resolution and did NOT re-promote any resolved entry. ## ★ B-LANGSTON-QUEUE open items (2026-06-22)

> Source: `Scope Files/B_LANGSTON_QUEUE_SCOPE.md` §6. Engine `comms-infra/discord/langston_queue.py` (39 unit tests) + `discord-langston-bridge.py` wiring; self-advance behind `LANGSTON_SELF_ADVANCE` (currently OFF). Langston Step-4 APPROVED 2026-06-22; his two findings (enqueue-too-broad, missing-marker-dead-locks-loop) FIXED + pushed (da02616d2). Live shakeout 2026-06-22 PASSED on those two + core auto-advance; surfaced the two gaps below.

- **#342 — B-LANGSTON-QUEUE OBJ-3 circle-back un-block NOT implemented (live-shakeout finding, 2026-06-22).** The *parking* half of skip-and-circle works (a blocked item is set aside, the loop skips it), but NOTHING flips a `blocked` item back to `ready` when its `want` dependency lands — and the marker grammar has no `ready` status. So a parked item stalls until manually re-marked; OBJ-3's "circle back the moment the dependency lands" is inert. Degrades safely (the item just waits, surfaced by the LOUD park notice), so it is a completeness gap, not a hazard — but B-LANGSTON-QUEUE is NOT fully functional until this lands (§9.1). **Proposed fix (Langston reviewing):** add `[[QUEUE id=X status=ready]]` so a CC (or Langston) can un-park when the dependency lands; optionally also auto-flip when a CC posts referencing the id. **HOME: B-LANGSTON-QUEUE go-live increment (Langston-reviewed Step-4 diff before flipping `LANGSTON_SELF_ADVANCE=1` for real).** **OPEN.**
- **#345 — B-LANGSTON-QUEUE: a real-inbound review gets ONE degraded self-advance re-pass (first live-use finding, 2026-06-22).** When a CC posts a review request, the bridge enqueues it (summary truncated to 500 chars, `pointer=None`) AND invokes Langston on the FULL message — but the real-inbound invocation prompt does NOT instruct the queue marker, so his first (complete) review carries no `done` marker → the item stays `ready` → the self-advance loop re-feeds it ONCE, now from the truncated summary with no pointer → Langston posts a shallower re-take (he flagged the truncation himself), emits the marker, item→done. Not a loop/hazard (it self-completes), but every review currently costs one wasted, degraded re-pass + a confusing shallow channel post. **Two fixes: (A root) append the queue-marker instruction to the real-inbound invocation when the message enqueued an item, so the FIRST review marks it done and there is NO re-pass; (B) capture a `pointer` at enqueue (regex an inbox/file path or commit sha out of the message) so any re-feed isn't degraded.** Surfaced on the build-crew-blueprint review (item `1518653167796097095`). **HOME: a small B-LANGSTON-QUEUE follow-up increment (Langston-reviewed). OPEN.**
  - **★ ESCALATED 2026-06-24 — the same defect, under sustained crew chatter, became an actual re-feed loop, and self-advance is now TEMPORARILY DISABLED.** During the B-ALERT batch's heavy CC↔Langston traffic, the `ready` conversational items (CC replies that START with "Langston", enqueued because `is_review_request` matched) were re-fed repeatedly, making Langston re-review already-settled topics over and over. **NEW root-cause finding (C):** the two-tier `CapTracker` runaway-brake **resets on every real Kyle/CC inbound** (`process_task`: `if not task.get("self_advance"): _cap.reset()`), so with continuous inbound the same-id halt NEVER accumulated to 2 → a stuck (ready, never-`done`) item re-fired indefinitely (observed: item `1519109514383720469` re-invoked at 22:56/22:57/23:01). **Stopgap applied 2026-06-24:** removed the systemd drop-in (`LANGSTON_SELF_ADVANCE` OFF, `daemon-reload` + restart) + cleared the 9-item ready backlog (marked `done`). Langston still answers DIRECT messages; only the auto-pickup is paused. **#345's fix now has THREE parts:** (A) append the queue-marker instruction to the real-inbound invocation so the FIRST review marks it done; (B) capture a `pointer` at enqueue; **(C) gate enqueue to GENUINE review-requests only (not conversational "Langston —" replies) AND make the same-id cap survive inbound resets (e.g. track same-id re-fires independently of the distinct-advance reset).** Re-enable `LANGSTON_SELF_ADVANCE=1` only after #345 ships Langston-reviewed. SIM "Langston self-advance loop" entry updated to the disabled state.
  - **✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-24 (B-LANGSTON-QUEUE-345, CC-A, deployed `907cd93db`).** All three fixes shipped + Langston Step-4 APPROVED: **C1** the enqueue gate now requires a review-REQUEST verb (`_REQUEST_VERB_RE`), so a discussion reply that merely says "verified/agreed" no longer enqueues (the bare-`verif` over-match is gone); **OBJ-B** a re-fetchable pointer (inbox path/repo file/sha) is captured at enqueue; **OBJ-A** the bridge auto-settles an enqueued item on any reply — `done` if Langston actually reviewed it, else a new `noop` terminal status (never falsely `done`) — so no degraded re-pass; **C2** a per-ITEM `self_advance_refires` counter persisted across save/load (Langston Step-4 caught a real persist bug — the increment was mutate-not-saved → the halt could never trip; fixed + an integration test that round-trips save/load now guards it). 79/79 unit tests. **Live shakeout PASSED:** self-advance re-enabled, a conversational probe containing "verified" did NOT enqueue (0 ready), zero churn re-invokes since re-enable. `LANGSTON_SELF_ADVANCE=1` is back on. SIM self-advance entry updated to the re-enabled state. **RESOLVED.**
- **#344 — B-LANGSTON-QUEUE: a marker-carrying CONTROL inbound was enqueued as a phantom work-item (shakeout-2 finding, 2026-06-22). ✅ FIXED same-day.** In shakeout-2 the un-park message ("Langston — `[[QUEUE id=UP1 status=ready]]` … please re-review") tripped `is_review_request` (it says "re-review") and got enqueued as a NEW item, which the loop then fed back to Langston (who correctly refused to confabulate a review of an unknown item). Fix: the enqueue gate also requires `not lq.marker_attempted(prompt)` — an inbound carrying a `[[QUEUE …]]` marker is a CONTROL message (un-park/re-mark), not a fresh review request. Tests 54→56. **HOME: folded into the go-live increment. RESOLVED (pending Langston quick-confirm + push).**
- **#343 — B-LANGSTON-QUEUE marker parser brittle to UNQUOTED want/reason (live-shakeout finding, 2026-06-22).** The `[[QUEUE …]]` regex requires `want="…"` / `reason="…"` to be QUOTED. In the shakeout Langston naturally emitted `want=shakeout-artifact` (unquoted) → the whole marker failed to parse → it fell through to the FINDING-2 park-unmarked path (so a genuine `blocked` was misclassified as "no marker"). Degraded SAFELY (loud park, nothing lost), but a stateless LLM will sometimes omit quotes. **Proposed fix (Langston reviewing): loosen the capture to accept an unquoted single token OR a quoted multi-word value for want/reason.** **HOME: same B-LANGSTON-QUEUE go-live increment as #342.** **OPEN.**

B79.TEC Step 4 hit this 4× consecutively on a 47KB diff review. Joint diagnosis with Langston (CLAUDE.md §6.7 peer-to-peer) → fix = `--output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages` to a sidecar NDJSON file; watchdog tails sidecar size for liveness; on clean exit `jq -r 'select(.type=="result") \| .result'` extracts assistant text into canonical OUTPUT_FILE. Timeouts retuned: first-byte 60s (system-init fires sub-second), idle 120s (tool-cycle gap). Sidecar rotation keeps last 10 in `/tmp/langston-stream/` for forensics. v1 backed up at `/usr/local/bin/langston-call.v1.backup`. Concurrent `crypto_spot` and `xstock_perp` flows continued normally. **Root cause:** Kraken WS-equities feed appears to send WS-protocol heartbeats only on weekends regardless of the 24/7 trading marker. **B79.0c shipped:** the per-symbol predicate + scanner universe-filter (the system handles 24/7 names correctly when WS data resumes). **What B79.0c did NOT do:** unblock the upstream feed silence. **Follow-up scope (B79.x candidate):** investigate alternative paths — (a) Kraken Pro account credentials may unlock a different feed-tier; (b) REST polling fallback (mirror B74's Kraken Futures REST pattern) using the equities REST endpoint if it exists; (c) direct probe to Kraken support/docs to confirm whether 24/7 WS data is meant to flow on weekends vs. is a feed-side limitation. Block on this before claiming "24/7 names live in production." | OPEN — B79.x follow-up | Documented in `BATCH_79_0c_COMPLETION_REPORT.md` §6 (WS investigation finding) + `CHANGES_AND_FIXES.md` INFRA-2026-05-09-E. | 105 | ✅ RESOLVED 2026-05-13 (BATCH_80) — TEC re-keyed by tradeId. Engine state per-trade. Option C+ rehydrate seed preserves in-flight `tradeMode` + `ladderRung` + `originalStopPrice` across restarts with engine-side defensive coercion for null-rung TRAILING_TAKE seeds. Runtime invariant `[B80][TEC_KEYING_INVARIANT_VIOLATION]` on every exit-cycle. 10 unit tests passing. Affects VTS + paper + live exit cycles. UI category line added in Open/Closed Simulated Trades per Kyle directive. PM2 #269 deployed; staging monitoring confirms zero invariant violations, multi-trade-per-symbol cases resolve correctly (XRP/GBP visibly has 2 independent trades with separate tradeIds + stops). See `Claude Comms and Packages/Batch Completion/BATCH_80_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`. **Original issue:** **TEC `trailingStates` keyed by SYMBOL instead of trade.id — concurrent trades on the same symbol share one engine state.** `server/services/trailing-exit-controller.ts` stores `trailingStates: Map<string, TrailingState>` keyed by symbol (lines 601, 613, 868, 870, 1098, 1108, 1172). VTS + paper + live trading paths all call `evaluateTECExit`/`updateTrailingState` and share the SAME map. **Multiple concurrent trades on the same symbol** (different strategies / different lanes — e.g., FET/USD with range_trade + support_bounce + morning_star open simultaneously per 2026-05-13 CSV export) **share ONE TEC state**. First trade to call `initializeTrailingState` sets the shared `currentStopPrice` + `originalStopPrice` + `highWaterMark`; subsequent trades inherit those values and the engine evaluates ALL of them against the FIRST trade's stop. When any one trade closes, `clearTrailingState(symbol)` (vts-runner.ts:2500, paper-execution-engine.ts:1352) wipes the shared state — next exit-cycle re-initializes from whichever surviving trade iterates first, so remaining trades on the symbol now follow a totally different trade's geometry. **Real-world impact:** displayed `trade.stopLoss` and engine `currentStopPrice` diverge for any trade after the first one on a given symbol. UI shows the trade's own per-trade stop; engine evaluates against the shared state's stop. Trades stay alive past their displayed stops (FET/USD range_trade in 2026-05-13 CSV: displayed stop 0.22863, engine stop 0.2117, current 0.225 → below displayed but above engine → still alive after 58h). BE-latch / target-latch / moonbag fires on one trade's geometry but the new floor applies to ALL concurrent trades on the symbol. Trailing HWM is conflated across trades. **Crypto duplicate-guard caps at 1 per (symbol, strategy) but allows different strategies → bug manifests in `quant-strong_trend` lane (vwap_pullback + strong_bull_trend co-eligible) and across regime transitions.** Active trading paper/live have no per-symbol cap → bug applies equally. **Fix proposal (B-NEW-24 / BATCH_80):** key `trailingStates` by trade.id (or positionId for paper/live). Plumb tradeId through `TECUpdate`. Update all reads + writes (getTrailingState, clearTrailingState, persistence migration). Recommend collapsing `trade.stopLoss` and `engine.currentStopPrice` into a single source of truth (UI Option C: display always = engine trigger). Needs full workflow with Langston design review BEFORE implementation per NO PATCHES doctrine (CLAUDE.md §5 #15). Bundled in BATCH_80 with the open-trades UI label change (crypto/xstock category line between symbol and asset class). | OPEN — needs design review + dedicated batch (BATCH_80) | Surfaced 2026-05-13 from FET/USD CSV export. Affects VTS + paper + live exit cycles. Root-cause analysis committed in tracker; design ask drafted in `Claude Comms and Packages/Langston Design Asks/B80_TEC_per_trade_keying_design_ask_rev1.md`. |

Either keeps the soak-verify script's authoritative log-grep signal untouched while making the live endpoint less alarming. | 116 | ✅ **PARTIALLY RESOLVED 2026-05-20 (B-NEW-36 sub-batch (b)) — xstock_spot fail-closed noise eliminated by side-effect; crypto_perp / xstock_perp still open.** Off-hours session-lifecycle controller now bulk-marks all open xStock VTS trades as `state='weekend_suspended'` during the Fri 8PM ET → Sun 8PM ET unified weekend close window. The VTS sim cycle `resolveOpenVirtualTrades` iteration filter (`if (t.state === 'weekend_suspended') continue;`) skips these trades, which means TEC `resolveTECConfig()` is NOT called for xstock_spot during the weekend window — eliminating the fail-closed log noise that was the primary observable failure. The underlying refresh-on-demand architecture remains; xstock_spot during ARCA hours, crypto_perp, and xstock_perp still rely on sporadic-consumer-driven refresh. If those classes' fail-closed noise becomes problematic, a separate background-timer batch can be opened (sister to #109's "stale-with-no-consumer" framing). Verification gate: zero `TEC_STALE_FAIL_CLOSED` events for asset_class=xstock_spot expected in the Fri 2026-05-22 8 PM ET → Sun 2026-05-24 8 PM ET window. Original entry: **TEC refresh-on-demand misses sporadic-consumer asset classes — fail-closed correctly fires but generates noise (extends #109).** Surfaced 2026-05-18 00:15 UTC ~2 hours after B-PHASE-A2 deploy (PM2 #294). `/api/diagnostics/tec-config` shows: crypto_spot HEALTHY (refreshed every ~60s, consumer = FX5 scanner cycle), but crypto_perp / xstock_spot / xstock_perp ALL `staleByCeiling: true`, lastSuccessAt = deploy time. **Root cause:** TEC config refresh is on-demand (triggered by `resolveTECConfig()` calls), not via independent background timer. Classes with continuous-rate callers (crypto_spot via FX5 scanner) stay fresh. Classes with sporadic callers (xstock_spot only via VTS sim cycle hitting open xStock virtual trades AND/OR xstock scanner runCycle when ARCA-open) go stale during quiet periods. Fence fires from `runPhase10SimulationCycle → resolveOpenVirtualTrades → evaluateTECExit → isMoonbagQualifier → resolveTECConfig` stack. **Operationally safe** — fence is the protection mechanism, working as designed (refuses to make decisions on stale config); zero money at risk (VTS sim is learning-only). NOT a B-PHASE-A2 regression (refresh-on-demand pattern predates A.2; same scenario would have fired on B-NEW-42b PM2 #293 had a VTS sim cycle hit it then). **Fix shape:** small batch — add a background timer in `tec-config-cache.ts` (or equivalent) that calls `primeTECConfig()` for all 4 active asset classes every 4 min regardless of consumer activity. Sister of #109's "stale-with-no-consumer" framing → this is "stale-with-sporadic-consumer where the fence actively fires." Until ship: each fire is a single log line; next deploy resets the cache; Mon ARCA-open xStock scanner activity will auto-refresh the xstock_spot cache (sustained-rate consumer). | PARTIALLY RESOLVED — B-NEW-36 (b) eliminates xstock_spot weekend instance; crypto_perp / xstock_perp residual still open | Surfaced 2026-05-18 00:15 UTC as live fail-closed event post-B-PHASE-A2 deploy. xstock_spot weekend instance closed via B-NEW-36 sub-batch (b) 2026-05-20. |
| 123 | **External macro feed (`external-macro-feed.ts`) is asset-class-agnostic.** Surfaced 2026-05-20 PM during B79.0n umbrella v1 "prove-me-wrong" audit (Langston item 13b). MCE has a "macro modifier" but the upstream macro feed itself doesn't differentiate per asset class. Crypto-relevant macro signals (BTC dominance, ETF flows, funding resets) are structurally different from xStock-relevant macro signals (rates, earnings calendar, macro econ releases). | 140 | **Deploy procedure refinement — apply migration BEFORE pm2 restart to eliminate fail-hard WARN window (Langston B79.0n.CONFIDENCE-CHAIN Step 8 observation, 2026-05-25).** During the B79.0n.CONFIDENCE-CHAIN Step 6 staging deploy at 17:51-17:55 UTC, the deploy procedure was: (1) git pull, (2) npm run db:migrate, (3) npm run build, (4) pm2 restart dawntrader. The migration step completed correctly but the live PM2 process was still running with the OLD code that didn't expect the new `b67_1_asset_class_no_op_active` constant. The MCE refresh cycle ticked every minute during the deploy window and hard-failed with `[B67.1][asset_class=crypto_spot] missing module_constants in macro_modifier module: b67_1_asset_class_no_op_active.` until the pm2 restart at 17:56 picked up the new code. This is BY-DESIGN behavior per §5 #10 (no silent fallback) + §5 #15 (NO PATCHES) — explicit retry on next timer tick is correct. But creates 2-6 minutes of WARN spam during deploys that add new module_constants rows. **Fix shape (Tier 3 polish, not blocking):** deploy-runbook entry that orders migration BEFORE the build/restart pair (already happens in current step order; the issue is that the OLD running process exercises the NEW constant requirement before the restart finishes). Alternative: pm2 stop dawntrader BEFORE npm run db:migrate, then run migration + build + pm2 start (cold-start variant). Out-of-batch fix candidate. | OPEN — Tier 3, deploy-procedure polish | Surfaced 2026-05-25 by Langston during B79.0n.CONFIDENCE-CHAIN Step 8 second-pass verification. |

| 135-ORIG | (Original 2026-05-22 surface, preserved for reference) Surfaced 2026-05-22 by Kyle. The system-alerts mechanism (`/var/log/dawntrader/system-alerts.jsonl` + the `fire-due` dispatcher cron) is a passive queue: the dispatcher promotes a scheduled alert to `state=active` (sets `fired_at`) when `triggers_at` passes — but it does NOT post to Telegram, message CC, or invoke Langston. The dispatcher log shows only `[fire-due] promoted N alert(s) to active`. The only "notification" path is the §10.5 per-turn pull check — every Claude Code session tails the alerts file and surfaces due entries. **Two coverage gaps:** (a) the §10.5 check only runs while a CC session is actively in progress — if no session is running when an alert fires, it waits unsurfaced until the next session starts; (b) Langston is structurally unable to catch a scheduled alert on his own — he is not a persistent process, he runs only when invoked via `claude -p`, so between invocations there is no Langston to run the §10.5 check. Empirical: d4b2e590 (2026-05-22 UD 24h regression) was caught only because CC was in an active session + Kyle surfaced a screenshot; Langston was not notified and could not have been. **Fix shape:** when the `fire-due` dispatcher promotes an alert to active it should also actively push — (1) post the alert to Telegram topic 21 via `cc-comms-bridge` so Kyle sees it, and (2) optionally invoke Langston via SSH+`claude -p` with the alert body so a Langston session actually runs and performs the §10.5 surfacing. Small, self-contained dispatcher enhancement — candidate for a quick standalone batch. | OPEN — Tier 3, alert-infra enhancement | Surfaced 2026-05-22 by Kyle question — the alert system did not actively notify CC or Langston of d4b2e590; the §10.5 pull-check is the sole path and has the two coverage gaps above. |

Does NOT enable `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` (catches `arr[i]` returning `T \| undefined`) or `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` (catches `{foo?: T}` accepting `{foo: undefined}`). Both are newer flags that catch additional related issues. Out of B79.0n.STORAGE scope but worth a future hardening sweep batch. | OPEN — Tier 3 hardening | Surfaced 2026-05-21 by CC pre-audit Concern C sub-finding for B79.0n.STORAGE. |

### #160 OPEN 2026-05-28 — TFS confidence-formula `momentumFactor` saturates above 1% xStock momentum (Phase 25 calibration input)
B-XSTOCK-CALIB B.1a archive replay (2,658 bars / 260 symbols) surfaced a structural characteristic of the TFS confidence formula on xStock data. The formula `confidence = tfsDesatMin + (tfsDesatMax - tfsDesatMin) × (momentumFactor × dbsStrength × volInverse)` computes `momentumFactor = clamp01(mom / tfsMomentumScale)` with xstock_spot's `tfsMomentumScale = 0.010`. So momentumFactor saturates at 1.0 once raw momentum exceeds 1%. xStock momentum p95 from the replay = 6.0%, so the saturation point is well below the 95th percentile of TFS-eligible bars. Practical effect: for the bulk of TFS-classified xStock bars, the formula's dynamic range collapses to `dbsStrength × volInverse` only — momentum is at the ceiling and not contributing to confidence differentiation. **Not a B.1 bug, not a B.1 fix** — the multiplicative formula structure with these scale values is intentional ("all three inputs must align" per B67.3.5 design). Phase 25 calibration cycle (when paper-trade outcomes are available) is the correct loop to decide whether momentumFactor saturation hurts trade selection — if high-confidence TFS labels predict winning trades only weakly, formula rescaling for xStock becomes justified at that point. Until then, capture as a structural observation. Full analysis in `Claude Comms and Packages/Cross-Session Briefs/B_1A_DISTRIBUTION_ANALYSIS.md` §2.1 + §5 Phase-25-handoff. | OPEN — Phase 25 calibration input | Surfaced 2026-05-28 during B-XSTOCK-CALIB B.1a archive replay.

### #162 ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-11 (B-4.7 chunk A) — Global REGIME is NOT per-class
**Resolution:** B-4.7 chunk A (deployed `b8ab812de`, 2026-06-11) deleted BOTH mixed-class dominant-regime votes (MCE + telemetry-aggregator) and replaced them with `getDominantRegimeForClass(assetClass)` (MIN 5 same-class pairs, null below = CLASS_IDLE); `getMarketIndicators(assetClass)` REQUIRED with per-class regime/friction/DBS and an explicit `voteStatus` LIVE|IDLE_OR_WARMING marker; per-class transition tracking with idle suppression + silent re-seed (independent friction idle flag per Langston diff-A R1); VTS stamps class-true `globalRegime`/`globalFriction`/`globalDirectionalBias` with at-open preservation (close-time re-resolution removed); per-class API payloads + two-badge UI.
