**#303 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6 Step-4 / Langston ask E — §13 concrete home) — the daily-loss-budget kill switch counts REALIZED 24h P&L only; a large open-position UNREALIZED drawdown never trips the kill** (the evaluator fires on a trade CLOSE, and `calculate24hPL` sums realized closed-trade P&L over the session-anchored 24h window — no mark-to-market of open positions). This is **FAITHFUL to the restored Phase-8 `risk-manager.ts::checkKillSwitch`** (its `calculate24hPL` `unrealizedPL` was a `// TODO` stub = 0 at `594aad717^`) and acceptable for the B6 restore, but it is a real gap: a single position bleeding hard in unrealized terms is invisible to the daily budget until it closes. **HOME (CONCRETE): Phase 25** — the unrealized extension needs (a) accrued real paper-run drawdown data to calibrate a sane unrealized threshold and (b) a mark-to-market price read on every evaluation (a periodic timer, not just on-close), both of which require active paper running (post-B7b). Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston Step-4 review 2026-06-17. | OPEN

**#238 OPEN 2026-06-14 (P19-B4a C5 / Langston Step-4 ratification condition) — default-open active strategy gate needs a per-class `strategy_gates` seed + a B7b pre-flight non-empty assertion before activation.** C5 disposed the hardcoded `enabledStrategies` allowlist and replaced it with the DB-resolved `isStrategyEnabledForAssetClass` gate, which is DEFAULT-OPEN (no `strategy_gates` row for a `(strategy, asset_class)` → enabled). Langston ratified default-open for B4a (the active path is dormant; explicit-allowlist would black out all crypto until a seed migration; the resolver throwing on cold cache is the real fail-hard). BUT default-open has a real activation edge: a freshly-onboarded asset class with ZERO `strategy_gates` rows runs EVERY strategy by default — for a thin, less-tested class that is uncontrolled exposure if seeding is forgotten. CURRENT STATE: `xstock_spot` IS fully seeded (19 rows: 10 enabled / 9 disabled, from `2026-05-11-b79-0m-a-xstock-strategy-gates-seeds.sql`); `crypto_spot` has ZERO rows → default-open-everything at activation. **HOME (Langston Step-4): B7b activation pre-flight.** Two deliverables before B7b switches a class on: (a) a `strategy_gates` seed migration establishing the INTENDED per-class enablement for `crypto_spot` (and re-confirm `xstock_spot`); (b) the B7b pre-flight MUST assert `strategy_gates` is NON-EMPTY for any asset class being switched on — so default-open can never silently green-light the full strategy set on a thin class. **TO FORMALIZE in PHASE_19_PLAN §5 (decision log) + §6 (B7b pre-flight gate).** | OPEN

**#320 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5a Step-2 / Langston ruling Q-B — §13 concrete home) — RTB defense-in-depth: whether the ready-to-buy admission must reject a queued signal whose asset class is gate-INACTIVE, on top of the B6.5a entry gates.** B6.5a applies the new per-(mode,asset_class) active gate at the two class ENTRY points only (`fx5-scanner.ts:543-555` crypto scan, `xstock_spot/active-dispatch.ts:122-123` xStock dispatch) — an inactive class never emits a signal, so RTB (`ready_to_buy_service.ts:593-594, 786-787`) should never receive one. Langston ruled entry-only is acceptable **CONDITIONAL**: B6.5b's accretion-delta audit MUST explicitly trace every RTB admission path and PROVE no signal can reach the queue past the entry gates. **HOME (CONCRETE, Langston Q-B): a P19-B6.5b deliverable** — if the audit proves the entry gates are the sole admission path, entry-only stands (with an explanatory comment left at the RTB admission sites noting the upstream guarantee); if the audit finds ANY path that injects past the entries, RTB per-signal `assetClass`-active enforcement goes in **as part of B6.5b, not "later."** Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston Step-2 review 2026-06-17. | OPEN

**#321 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5a Step-4 / Langston Q1 — §13 concrete home) — `tradingStateSync.witnessAssetClassEmissionWhileInactive(mode,class)` is a defined-but-UNCALLED "hard witness" method.** B6.5a shipped it as the Q-D hard-breach observability hook (records a `LIVENESS_SPLIT` if a class emits active output while its gate is OFF), but it has **no call site** — by construction the entry gate prevents emission-while-OFF, so there is nowhere to wire it YET. Per §15 an uncalled method should not linger. Langston (Step-4) ruled it acceptable to ship ONLY because B6.5b is the exact place a real call site appears: the accretion-delta audit that hunts for an ALTERNATE emission path (one that bypasses the entry gates) is what would instrument the breach. **HOME (CONCRETE, Langston Q1): P19-B6.5b deliverable** — during B6.5b's audit, either (a) WIRE the method at the alternate emission path the audit identifies, OR (b) DELETE it if the audit proves the entry gates are the sole emission path. **★Also captured for B6.5b notes (Langston Q3): the dual-active edge** — `fx5-scanner` reads only the RESOLVED active mode's context for the crypto gate (fine today since one mode is active at a time; but if live+paper are ever BOTH ON, the crypto gate would consult only the live-resolved context). Record in the B6.5b audit so it is on file. Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston Step-4 review 2026-06-17. **★REFRAME (Kyle directive 2026-06-17): the dual-active case is NOT an edge case — it is the INTENDED STEADY STATE.** Once live launches, paper mode keeps running CONTINUOUSLY alongside live as the calibration/new-functionality testing ground (paper = "VTS that goes through the FULL pipeline" — SQE emits one best signal per cycle, not one per strategy). Both modes active simultaneously was the DRIVING FORCE behind the paper/live cleave (the between-Phase-24-and-19 work; B4b D5 isolation). So the `fx5-scanner` single-`activeMode`-per-tick scan loop AND this per-class gate's single-context read BOTH must handle concurrent paper+live — a **Phase-21 co-run precondition** (alongside the B4b D5 `LIVENESS_SPLIT` 0-during-co-run gate). **HOME re-pointed: Phase-21 live+paper co-run** (B6.5b is paper-ONLY so it only RECORDS this, does not fix it). | OPEN

**#322 OPEN 2026-06-17 (Kyle directive — live-mode-creation requirement; §9.4 home) — "test-in-paper / bypass-in-live" capability gating.** Live and paper will share the SAME pipeline except at the final order-placement seam (P19-B2 Option-A reuse-by-extension). Since paper is the permanent testing ground for new functionality + calibration while live runs real money, **new/experimental functionality must be runnable in PAPER but BYPASSABLE in LIVE** — a per-capability feature-gate keyed by mode (test in paper, skip in live until proven), so unproven logic never touches live orders. NOT a build now; a forward architectural requirement Kyle wants captured. **HOME (CONFIRMED, Kyle directive 2026-06-17): P19-B15** — a dedicated Phase-19 batch, scheduled LAST in the Phase-19 board (it pairs with no existing batch; Kyle: "give it a confirmed scheduled place in Phase 19 … if it doesn't fit with anything else, save it until last"). Design to be brought to Langston when B15 is scoped. Relates to #321 (both-modes steady state) + the per-class gate (B6.5a, the first such mode-keyed gate). | OPEN

**#323 OPEN 2026-06-17 (Kyle directive — daily-loss failsafe philosophy; revisit-conditional home) — auto-trip vs alert-only vs user-configurable for the daily-loss budget.** Kyle is weighing whether the automatic daily-loss kill (P19-B6) should be a hard auto-trip at all, vs an **alert-only** mode (the user is alerted at loss thresholds and decides whether to stop the system themselves), vs a user-configurable choice between the two — the principle being the user should have a say in how much they're willing to risk, even though most of the system is automated/protected. **DECISION FOR NOW: keep the auto-trip at the 20% paper cutoff (Kyle 2026-06-17).** **HOME: revisit — CONDITIONAL trigger = "if the failsafe trips too often during paper-mode testing."** Tie-in: P19-B6.8 (per-mode guardrail completeness) is the natural place to add an alert-only / auto-trip user toggle if pursued. Recorded so the consideration isn't lost. | OPEN

**#324 OPEN 2026-06-17 (B-GOV Step-4 / Langston — §9.4 concrete home; HARD pre-activation gate) — B-GOV-2: the governance-checker must NOT be flipped to a live always-on watcher until two gaps close.** The B-GOV batch (commit `3d3dce073`) shipped the checker as INERT scaffolding (runs on demand only — the backtest). Two items HARD-GATE enabling the systemd timer: **(1) change-class declaration + Obj-12 path-heuristic** — `computeBatchStates` never sets `declaredClass`, so every batch falls to `DEFAULT_CLASS = architecture`, which requires `system_manual` + `sim` on everything → false doc-gap REDs on every sub-batch/non-arch close if run live; the class must be declared (scope-header) + a cheap diff-path under-declaration guard before live. **(2) dead-man heartbeat** — `HEARTBEAT_MISS_LIMIT` is defined-but-unwired; `Persistent=true` only gives boot catch-up, not silence-detection; a silently-dead checker = zero enforcement with nobody told (the §18 failure mode). **HOME (CONCRETE): dedicated batch `B-GOV-2`, runs BEFORE the timer is ever enabled.** C1 (DELETED_COMPONENTS_LOG conditional) stays a safe deferral (can never RED-alarm). The poller stays INERT until B-GOV-2 lands, so the architecture-default false-RED problem cannot fire in production. Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston Step-4 review 2026-06-17. **★UPDATE 2026-06-19 — B-GOV-2 (`ec37b2990`) ADDRESSED both original gates:** change-class declaration (OBJ-1 `readDeclaredClass` from the scope header, fail-closed) + dead-man heartbeat (OBJ-3, separate `governance-checker-heartbeat` service+timer) shipped, tested (35/35), installed on staging. The checker is now **activated-in-shadow but PAUSED** (timers disabled) after its first tick flooded the §10.5 queue with 88 activation-day backfill entries (all resolved). **TWO NEW follow-ups now gate live-paging** (entries below — exception seeding + shadow-surfacing). #324's two original pre-activation gates are MET. | ADDRESSED (checker activated-in-shadow; live-paging gated on the 2 follow-ups below)

**#325 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run / §9.4 concrete home; BLOCKS gate-10 + B7b) — TCL→paper-execution-engine open-path silent failure: a sized crypto active-paper signal never opens a paper trade.** The B6.5c repair (the `cwqi` DB-drift DROP + the pattern→canonical-strategy resolve) cleared both ready-to-buy-insert breaks, so crypto signals now reach ready-to-buy with valid canonical names — but the FULL closed-trade lifecycle (B6.5c objective 5 / gate-10) STILL does not complete: a sized signal falls out somewhere in the TCL→paper-execution-engine handoff WITHOUT opening a position. The system's own open-path invariant monitor `[8.8.3-I3]` reports the diagnostic signature **attempts > 0, opened = 0, blocked = 0, reasonSum = 0** (observed attempts=11/opened=0/blocked=0/reasonSum=0 in the reverted dry-run) — i.e. the signal is counted as an open ATTEMPT but falls out BEFORE any block-reason or telemetry is recorded, which points at an un-awaited promise / a swallowed throw / an early-return-with-no-telemetry inside the TCL→PEE open path (this path has been untested since Phase 8). Surfaced by the P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run. **HOME (CONCRETE): dedicated batch `P19-B6.5e` — "TCL→paper-execution-engine open-path silent-failure repair + gate-10 closed-lifecycle home,"** which OWNS gate-10 / the ≥1 FULL closed-trade-lifecycle proof (open→exit→close→cooldown→telemetry); may promote to a full `P19-B7` if the open-path surface is large. **B7b stays HARD-GATED on the closed-lifecycle proof, now owned by B6.5e** (PHASE_19_PLAN §6 gate 10). Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston ruling 2026-06-17. **★RESOLVED 2026-06-18 (P19-B6.5e — the silent failure is fixed; the open path executes correctly).** ROOT CAUSE confirmed (Step-1.a): `executeSimulatedTrade` was `void`-returning with ~7 bare-`return` post-guardrail early-exits invisible to the I3 invariant, and `executePromotedSignal` inferred success by a trade-count delta — so any post-guardrail open failure produced "no new trade" with no error / no reason (the `reasonSum=0` signature). FIX: a typed `OpenOutcome` return + a NEW `openFailedTotal` / `openFailedByStage` third term in `rtb-metrics-service` (the I3 invariant is now `attempts === opened + blocked + openFailed`; recorded via `recordOpenFailed(symbol,strategy,stage,reason)`). PROVEN LIVE on the deployed code (`dbd0a2283`, CI `27731034448` all-4-green, staging restart#404): the contained crypto dry-run reconciled the invariant and NAMED the stage — `[8.8.3-I3][INVARIANT_CHECK][OK] attempts=8 opened=0 blocked=0 openFailed=8 [EV_REJECT:8]` — so a sized signal can never silently vanish again. **The open is NOT broken in execution:** it reaches the Net-Expectancy gate (11.8B), which HONESTLY rejects every crypto signal (NetEV<0 after friction) because the EV kernel runs on DEFAULT pWin=0.60/DI=50/VolNoise=0.300 inputs (the already-homed #233 — fixing the EV inputs, NOT the open path, is the lever for a real open). gate-10 (the ≥1 full closed crypto lifecycle proof) accordingly MOVED to the new batch P19-B6.5g (EV-input integrity + lifecycle proof); B7b stays hard-gated on B6.5g-green. Langston Step-1 PROCEED + Step-4 APPROVE (Phase A). | RESOLVED

**#326 OPEN 2026-06-17 (P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run / §9.4 concrete home — B63 DBS-propagation lineage; NON-FATAL) — B63 DBS-not-propagated hard-contract violation on some crypto pairs in the signal-orchestrator pattern-pool eval.** In the pattern-pool branch of the signal orchestrator, when a pair object lacks a `dbsScore`, `propagatedDbs` comes through undefined and the MCE throws the **B63 hard contract** (DBS-must-be-propagated). It is caught as a `[14.5][ORCHESTRATOR]` warn and those pairs are SKIPPED — so it is **non-fatal** (it does not crash the cycle), but it silently drops affected crypto pairs from the pattern-pool eval. This is a pre-existing B63-era issue separate from the B6.5c repair (NOT folded into B6.5c — certainty-before-cutting; it belongs to the DBS-propagation lineage, not the pattern→strategy / `cwqi` breaks). Surfaced by the P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run. **HOME (CONFIRMED 2026-06-17, CC + Langston consensus per §9.4): its OWN named pre-go-live item — "P19 pre-go-live DBS-propagation hardening" — GATED BEFORE B7b crypto activation** (trace why some pattern-pool pairs reach the orchestrator without a `dbsScore`; guarantee DBS propagation so no crypto pair is silently skipped under active trading). Considered folding into the #200 / #222 DBS lineage but kept STANDALONE — different surface (the propagation PATH, not writer-hygiene/config). Surfaced by the P19-B6.5c gate-10 dry-run; home confirmed via the B6.5c Step-8 exchange (Langston flagged "proposed≠confirmed", CC agreed standalone-item — converged, no Kyle escalation). | OPEN

**A/EUR active-path classify fall-through (alert `58367b27`) ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-18 (P19-B6.5d).** The live `classify-fallthrough-active` CRITICAL alert (id `58367b27`, `A/EUR@kraken` dropped on the active path) was the trigger for P19-B6.5d. ROOT CAUSE: the resolver's base-length regexes had an implicit FLOOR of 2 capital letters, so the single-letter base `A` (= Vaulta, a real Kraken-spot crypto) fell through to the classify-failure path and the pair was dropped. FIX (P19-B6.5d OBJ-1): widened the floor 2→1 via ONE SSOT constant `TICKER_BASE_MIN_LEN=1` (`shared/asset-classes.ts`) feeding all 3 resolver regexes, with collision precedence preserved (resolver order DISPLAY→COLLISIONS→SYMBOLS→CANONICAL→raw — so the widen can never let a single-letter crypto shadow an `XSTOCK_SPOT_KRAKEN_COLLISIONS` ticker). **Verified on the deployed resolver:** `A/EUR`=crypto_spot, `T/USD`=crypto_spot (collision gate, drift-warn), `Tx/USD`=xstock_spot. **Alert `58367b27` is RESOLVED.** Note this is the SPECIFIC single-letter-base fall-through; the broader active-path `resolveAssetClass` hardening (the carry-the-stamp invariant across the resolve sites) also landed in P19-B6.5d and the active-path safe-conversion/escalation-hook work tracked under #228/#230 stays as-homed. Commit `3bd3deedc`, CI `27728040408` all-4-green, Langston Step-4 APPROVE + Step-8 CONFIRMED-CLOSED. | RESOLVED

**P19-B6.5f OPEN 2026-06-18 (NEW batch — urgent, ships FIRST; P19-B6.5e dry-run finding; §9.4 concrete home) — canonicalizer quote-currency completeness: the newer Kraken stablecoin quote currencies fall through active-path classify.** The P19-B6.5e crypto dry-run fired **6 critical `classify-fallthrough-active` alerts** — ETH/EUROP, ETH/PYUSD, XBT/EUROP, XBT/PYUSD, XRP/RLUSD (+ the earlier A/EUR). **Two confirmed root causes (both QUOTE-side — DISTINCT from B6.5d's single-letter BASE fix):** (a) `symbol-canonicalizer.ts:151` `knownQuotes` is missing the newer Kraken stablecoin quotes (EUROP / PYUSD / RLUSD); (b) `shared/asset-classes.ts` `CRYPTO_SPOT_CANONICAL` quote group `[A-Z0-9]{3,4}` caps the quote at 4 chars while EUROP / PYUSD / RLUSD are 5 chars. FIX: enumerate Kraken's full current quote set (verify live) + widen the quote-length bound + add a **loud named alert on any unrecognized quote** so the next gap is a one-line diagnosis. **HOME (CONCRETE): new batch `P19-B6.5f`, sequenced FIRST (before B6.5g).** Surfaced + homed by CC + Langston (root-cause analysis contributed by Claude Old + Langston, Kyle-relayed) 2026-06-18. **★RESOLVED 2026-06-19 (P19-B6.5f / reorg-B1 — RE-SCOPED both-class per reorg D1/D2).** FIX (code `b06eb9e5c`, CI `27837279938` all-4-green, deployed staging restart, bench tsc-baseline-clean + vitest 128/128): (a) a shared `QUOTE_LEN_MIN/MAX` SSOT (MAX=5 = longest live leg, data-confirmed from live `/0/public/AssetPairs` = 23 distinct legs) widened `CRYPTO_SPOT_CANONICAL` + `XSTOCK_SPOT_DISPLAY` {3,4}→{3,5} in lockstep — this alone closes the 6 slash-form alerts; (b) a complete curated `KNOWN_QUOTE_CURRENCIES` (23 legs) + a server-injected self-healing `setDiscoveredQuotes()` slot (fed from `kraken-asset-pairs-service.dynamicQuotes` at the tail of EVERY refresh; SIM §17/S17) replaced the hand-maintained narrow lists across the recognition-decision sites (canonicalizer compact-split + raw-form regexes); (c) a dedicated STORM-SAFE `classify-unknown-quote:${exchange}` alert names the unrecognized quote. **PROVEN LIVE on deployed code:** all 6 pairs (ETH/EUROP, ETH/PYUSD, XBT/EUROP, XBT/PYUSD, XRP/RLUSD, A/EUR) → crypto_spot; xStock verified complete (490-sym universe, AAPL/AAPLx/equities all xstock_spot, SUI collision→crypto); genuinely-unknown quote still fail-closes. **Recognition≠eligibility CONFIRMED:** universe-loader `allowedQuotes`=[USD,USDT,USDC] unchanged → exotic-quote pairs recognized-not-traded. ALSO surface the `openFailedByStage` / `byReason` breakdown in `GET /api/diagnostics/rtb-metrics` (see the rtb-metrics API field-gap note below). **gate-10 MOVED here from B6.5e** (it never vanishes — it moves); **B7b activation stays HARD-GATED on B6.5g-green.** (Naming note: Langston suggested "P19-B7"; renamed B6.5g to avoid colliding with the existing B7a/B7b activation batches and keep gate-10 in the crypto-resurrection arc.) **reorg-B2 surfaced items (2026-06-20, CC-B + Langston Step-4/Step-8 consensus on Discord) — concrete homes (§9.4):**
> **Issue-number deconfliction (2026-06-20):** these were originally homed #332/#333/#334 but #332/#333 COLLIDED with B-DISCORD's #332/#333 (the two concurrent CC sessions independently grabbed the same numbers — a shared-state hazard of the per-session split). Renumbered here: win-rate #332→**#335**, xStock-floor #333→**#336**; #334 (V4#2) kept (no collision); friction_safety_buffer = NEW **#337**.
- **#335 — Realized net-of-friction win-rate validation BEFORE crypto active-trading turns on (HOME: P19 reorg-B9 turn-on pre-flight gate).** At the Tier-1 taker fee wall (~1.8% round-trip) with pWin capped 0.60, the EV kernel is net-negative even at a 4% target / 1.6% stop (0.6·4 − 0.4·1.6 − 1.8 ≈ −0.04%), so the 11.8B EV gate honestly refuses to open crypto at taker. reorg-B2 ships the rung-1 PLUMBING; the EV gate is the safety. **Before B9 flips crypto active-paper ON, pull the realized net-of-friction win rate per regime and confirm it clears the breakeven (~58-63% at these values); if not, taker-rung-1 cannot open profitably.** The real opener = the maker build (reorg-B7, rung-2) + the pWin-ceiling recalibration (Phase-25). Decision-grade; matches the Phase-19 audit's fee-wall conclusion.
- **#336 — xStock per-class target floor must come DOWN (HOME: Phase-25 calibration item 25-17 ↔ this issue).** reorg-B2 seeds xStock target_floor_pct = 0.040 (= crypto, same account-wide fee wall) as a placeholder. A 4% intraday floor will under-trade xStock (equities don't move 4% intraday like alts). Phase-25 calibrates xStock's floor + reach_atr_max DOWN on its own realized data. NOT a verbal "Phase-25 nice-to-have" — a named Phase-25 calibration item (Langston #5).
- **#334 — reorg-B2 V4 literal #2: the pattern-path stop/target fallback (`signal-orchestrator.ts` `?? currentPrice * 0.97 / * 1.03`) (HOME: P19 pre-go-live cleanup batch).** V4 #1 (the `?? entry×1.015` target fallback in buildSizedSignalForStrategy) was deleted on the spot. #2 fabricates a sub-fee-wall stop/target when a pattern tradeSignal lacks geometry; removal is coupled to the sizing-requires-a-stop dependency (sizing runs before the normalizer's geometry-drop), so it gets a dated home rather than a risky in-batch removal: a pattern signal lacking geometry should DROP, not fabricate. Coordinate the removal with the sizing pre-check in the Phase-19 pre-go-live cleanup.
- **#337 — `expectancy_gates.friction_safety_buffer` per-class evaluation (HOME: Phase-25 calibration item 25-18 ↔ this issue; surfaced by Langston Step-8 2026-06-20).** reorg-B2 split the 6 ROI gate knobs per-class but kept `friction_safety_buffer` a single global `'*'` row BY DESIGN — it is a uniform safety **margin** on top of the friction **model**, and the model is already per-class (`fee_model` + per-class spreads carry the crypto-vs-xStock fee/spread/settlement differences), so a uniform margin on top is correct, not a missed split (CC-B + Langston consensus). **TRIGGER (explicit, §13):** once the per-class friction MODELS are calibrated in Phase-25, evaluate whether the buffer ITSELF should differ per-class (i.e. whether the calibrated per-class friction profiles leave a residual the uniform margin mis-sizes). Until then it stays global. Roadmap item carries this trigger verbatim. **OPEN (intentional-global; Phase-25 revisit).**
- **#338 — Signal-target-normalizer PLACEMENT: move the gates into the strategies' shared guard + DROP the floor-lift (HOME: NEW named P19 sub-batch `reorg-B2.1`; CC-B + Langston converged 2026-06-21, Kyle-requested review; PENDING Kyle go-ahead to schedule/build).** Kyle questioned whether reorg-B2's three normalizer ops belong in a post-hoc helper vs at signal generation. CC-B investigated all 19 strategies (`Langston Design Asks/REORG_B2_NORMALIZER_PLACEMENT_REVIEW_2026-06-21.md`); CC-B + Langston joint conclusion: **(a) DROP the floor-LIFT entirely** — it's a target MUTATION (split-brain: strategy's chosen target ≠ what executes) AND redundant with the **Net-Expectancy (11.8B) gate**, which already enforces beat-friction per-signal (RR×min-stop = 0.75% implied ≪ the 4% floor, so the floor was never doing reward/risk work — it was a coarse EV-gate proxy). **(b) CONSOLIDATE the RR gate to ONE per-class SSOT** in `strategy-helpers.applyGlobalGuards` — TODAY a live split-brain (helpers hardcode RR≥1.5, normalizer re-gates at per-class 2.5, stricter silently wins, the helper's guard becomes dead code misreporting the threshold — a NO-PATCHES violation in production). **(c) MOVE reachability** into the shared guard (formula-agnostic). **(d) WIRE the ~9 in-class detectors** (in `strategy-engine.ts`) into `applyGlobalGuards` (they bypass it today) → gates at signal generation + DRY + VTS auto-coverage (VTS calls `detect*` directly). **(e) SQE rejected as home** (downstream, also sees signal in isolation). **SEQUENCING (Langston, load-bearing): do NOT retire the normalizer first** — it is currently the ONLY RR/reachability coverage for the in-class strategies + VTS; it stays as the BRIDGE and retires LAST, once shared-guard coverage has moved. **TWO BLOCKING pre-pull gates before dropping the floor-lift (Langston):** (1) prove NO downstream consumer keys off the LIFTED target value (RTB pool, TEC sizing, diagnostics, VTS logging) — or migrate it in the same sub-batch; (2) confirm the NetEV gate genuinely fires on EVERY path the normalizer guarded (incl. any VTS-only/cold-start path) — else dropping the floor opens an ungated hole. **Filter-Diagnostics visibility** (Kyle's hard no-hidden-gates requirement, both VTS tabs) folds in here, sequenced to read from the shared guard (not built against the soon-retired helper). Also fixes the normalizer's wrong "21 strategies" comment → 19. **OPEN — Kyle GREENLIT 2026-06-21 (full workflow autonomously w/ Langston); scope `P19_REORG_B2_1_SCOPE.md` + pre-audit `P19_REORG_B2_1_PRE_AUDIT.md` committed, Langston Step-1 PROCEED, Step-2 in review. Both pre-pull gates verified clear (1a clean, 1b satisfied-w-nuance: active strict NetEV / VTS relaxed by design).**
- **#370 — Validate the Net-Expectancy gate's JUDGMENT QUALITY (does it actually select the trades that end up profitable?) — Kyle 2026-06-21 (HOME: Phase-25 calibration item 25-19 ↔ this issue).** reorg-B2.1 makes the Net-Expectancy gate the cost-coverage judge (drops the crude 4% floor). Kyle: "we have to look at its judgment skills — its ability to actually judge." The validation needs paper-active outcomes, so it's a Phase-25 item. **BASELINE = the VTS 4%-target study (`VTS_4PCT_TARGET_STUDY_2026-06-21.md`):** a flat 4% target does NOT predict crypto profitability (≥4% win 31% vs 33% for <4%), so the EV gate must do better than a crude proxy; xStock's 40-vs-26 win-rate split on target quality is a real (gross/confounded) signal the gate + calibration should capture. **Method (Phase-25):** measure whether NetEV-passing trades realize positive net-of-friction outcomes per regime/class vs NetEV-rejected; recalibrate the kernel inputs (pWin/DI/VolNoise — see #233) if its judgment is poor. ↔ POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP 25-19. **OPEN (Phase-25).**
- **#371 — reorg-B2.1 OBJ-5 guard-equivalence DELTA: measure GUARD-5 reachability over-rejection vs the retiring normalizer (HOME: reorg-B2.1 OBJ-5, the normalizer-retirement gate; CC-B + Langston Step-4 2026-06-21).** OBJ-2/3 added `validateReachability` (GUARD-5) to `applyGlobalGuards` using the strategy's `getEffectiveATR` (clamped) — while the normalizer's reachability uses `mceContext.indicators.atr`. Until the normalizer retires (OBJ-5), GUARD-5 is a NEW intersection gate running IN ADDITION to it, so the risk is **one-directional over-rejection** where `effectiveATR < mceContext.atr` makes `atrsToTarget` larger → a signal GUARD-5 drops that the normalizer would have passed. **OBJ-5 precondition (Langston): MEASURE this delta on staging (the two ATR sources + the RR net-neutrality are the two load-bearing identities of the equivalence proof) — not assert near-equivalence — before retiring the normalizer.** If the delta is material, reconcile the ATR source (use the same ATR in both, or accept the guard's effectiveATR as canonical with evidence). **SCOPE (widened OBJ-4b 2026-06-21): this divergence covers ALL guard-wired in-class strategies — including the 3 non-ATR-geometry ones (sma_trend_ride, vwap_bounce, dhma) which feed the reachability a `computeATR(priceHistory)` pair-ATR (their geometry is SMA/VWAP/realizedVol-based; reachability is a path-invariant PAIR property — Langston) — NOT only the ATR-native strategies. Same divergence class (guard-clamped ATR vs normalizer `mceContext.atr`); one measurement at OBJ-5 covers all of them, no second ticket.** **OBJ-5 IMPLEMENTATION NOTE (Langston Step-4 §13): `recordGuardEval` captures rr/pass/dropReason but NOT the `effectiveATR`/`atrsToTarget` values — so the OBJ-5 guard-vs-normalizer ATR correlation MUST add `effectiveATR` (+ `atrsToTarget`) capture to the tracker (and the normalizer-side `mceContext.atr` capture) before the divergence can be measured. Written here so it's not lost.** **OPEN (reorg-B2.1 OBJ-5).**
- **#372 — Per-strategy minRR calibration: the reorg-B2 `min_rr=2.5` seed SUPPRESSES most of the strategy suite (HOME: Phase-25, pairs with 25-2/3/10 / #336; INSTRUMENTATION PRECURSOR in reorg-B2.1 OBJ-4; CC-B + Langston 2026-06-21).** Per-strategy analysis (`Scope Files/REORG_B2_1_OBJ4_PER_STRATEGY_ANALYSIS_2026-06-21.md`): the per-class `min_rr=2.5` is higher than most strategies' DESIGNED reward-to-risk, so the normalizer drops them today (VTS). Hard-suppressed (fixed RR<2.5, every signal): strong_bull_trend (6/3 ATR=2.0), vwap_bounce (2R=2.0), sma break-leg (2R=2.0), dhma (~1.0); runtime-suppressed-prone: orb (~1.3), breakout (<1.5), range_trading, mean_reversion, abcd_long. reorg-B2 RAISED the floor 1.5(file-based)/none(in-class)→2.5(all via the normalizer) — that introduced it. **Langston framing: the real gate is Net-Expectancy, not RR in isolation — a 2.0-RR strategy at a healthy win rate is positive-EV; a blanket 2.5 floor that kills it DESTROYS EV, not improves quality. Per-strategy minRR (not a 2.5 wildcard) is almost certainly the end state (the per-class-not-wildcard rule points there).** **PRECURSOR (reorg-B2.1 OBJ-4, NOT "lower it later"): instrument the per-strategy guard-drop `rr` over a VTS window + the guard-vs-normalizer divergence (one log, two measurements) → bring Kyle DATA-BACKED numbers, not a blind pick.** Then Kyle's philosophy call (high-RR-only vs trade-as-designed / per-strategy minRR), measured as EV-preserving. **OPEN (Kyle decision pending the instrumentation; Phase-25 home).**
  - **★ 48h WINDOW READ 2026-06-23 (clean: pm2 uptime 48.2h, restart#408, no mid-window wipe; CC-B pulled `/api/diagnostics/guard-eval-stats` live).** The global `min_rr=2.5` wildcard suppresses **62–100% of every recorded strategy** (11 of 17 guard-active had signals; 6 none). 100%: strong_bull_trend (1433/0, meanRR 2.00), vwap_bounce (256/0, 2.00), defensive_hedge (28/0, 1.22); sma_trend_ride 99.7% (2099, 2.00); support_bounce 98.8% (496, 0.85); morning_star 98.4% (**13638 evals**, 0.97); volatility_edge 95.8% (1.48); reverse_impulse 76.8% (1.88); pivot_shift 66.3% (2.23); vwap_pullback 65.7% (4649, 2.38, only reachDrops 1530); range_trade 62.4% (2.35). **Kyle AGREED with the per-strategy baseline → calibrate-from-win-rates path (2026-06-23).** **Langston 3 refinements (2026-06-23, accepted):** (1) global 2.5 = textbook §8 #11 wildcard violation (strangles the 3 highest-conviction trend strategies); minRR is a coarse proxy for Net-Expectancy (the real judge). (2) **set minRR a notch BELOW each strategy's meanRR, NOT at it** — anchoring AT the mean suppresses ~50% of its own distribution by construction; floor below the center to trim only genuinely-thin setups. (3) **per-class KEYING from day one** — a per-strategy wildcard is STILL a wildcard across classes (§8 #11); key the knob `(strategy × asset_class)` in `module_constants` from day one even though only crypto RR data exists now. **★ KYLE DECISION 2026-06-23 (overrides Langston's "xStock inherits crypto placeholder"): do NOT inherit crypto — set xStock's OWN baseline from xStock's OWN per-class data; a thin-data xStock strategy gets a conservative default, NOT a crypto borrow.** **★ CONCRETE HOMES (Kyle directive "say WHERE, not just homed"): the BASELINE-SET = NEW Phase-19 batch `reorg-B2.3`** (migrate the minRR knob global→per-`(strategy×asset_class)` + set each baseline a notch BELOW each strategy's meanRR from each class's OWN per-class VTS data; after reorg-B2.2's OBJ-6 re-key supplies per-class data); **the WIN-RATE RECALIBRATION = roadmap `25-20`** (Phase-25, post-turn-on outcomes). **★ PER-CLASS RETRO-SPLIT NOT POSSIBLE (CC-B verified 2026-06-23):** guard drops are strategy-only counters (no symbol/class stored per drop); `skipped-signals-logger` (carries symbols) does NOT capture the guard RR/reachability drops (the strategy guard short-circuits upstream — the only window per-signal records are `Net_EV_Negative`). So the per-class split needs OBJ-6's re-key captured GOING FORWARD (reorg-B2.2). **★ SEQUENCING (Langston §13):** the WIN-RATE calibration needs active-paper OUTCOMES → gated behind Phase-19 turn-on; the RR-distribution BASELINE can be set now from VTS. Keep #372's win-rate half sequenced AFTER Phase 19, not floating.

OBJ-5 retires the normalizer — but ONLY after a 3-condition gate, ALL required, THEN cut: **(1) #371 ATR-capture LIVE** — add `effectiveATR` + `atrsToTarget` capture to `recordGuardEval`/the tracker (+ the normalizer-side `mceContext.atr` capture) so the guard-vs-normalizer divergence is measurable; **(2) SUPPRESSION + DIVERGENCE MEASURED over a real window** — anchor: a **≥48h VTS accumulation window** with the tracker **sanity-checked non-zero first** (a zero-that-never-moves = a wiring bug that would silently null #372/#371 — confirm `GET /api/diagnostics/guard-eval-stats` populates per active strategy before trusting any delta — **✅ SANITY MET 2026-06-21: tracker populated 3 strategies / 23 evals within 90s of deploy, so the wiring records; the ≥48h window is now accumulating real data**); **(3) #371 DIVERGENCE QUANTIFIED as acceptable** (guard clampEffectiveATR vs normalizer mceContext.atr one-directional over-rejection bounded). Only with all three → retire the normalizer (§15/§18 disposition: DELETED_COMPONENTS_LOG + `_archive/.removed` + tsc-traced no dangling ref) + fold in the 2 Langston nits (clampEffectiveATR-as-getEffectiveATR-base DRY; SBT import-extension consistency). **OPEN (reorg-B2.1 OBJ-5; gate not yet met — window accumulating).** **★ WINDOW IS FRAGILE — the `guard-eval-tracker` is IN-MEMORY, so ANY staging restart/deploy RESETS it and restarts the window (Langston + CC-B 2026-06-21). If a restart does occur, the window restarts (the suppression RATE re-stabilizes once enough evals re-accumulate — not catastrophic, just resets the clock). A future hardening = persist the counters; out of scope for this window.** **★ UNPLANNED-RESTART DETECTION (Langston 2026-06-21 — a crash/OOM/host-reboot zeroes the window with nobody pinged): THIS window uses ZERO-CODE detection — at read-time (window close), VERIFY no staging restart occurred during the window before trusting any number (check `pm2 list` uptime ≥ window age + the server boot log for a mid-window restart; if one occurred, the window was reset → re-anchor, do NOT read partial counters as a full window). A code stamp now would itself reset the window, so the PROPER hardening — persist/checkpoint the counters OR expose a `trackerStartedAt` stamp in `getGuardEvalStats` so a wipe is self-evident — rides with the OBJ-5/6 post-window diff (which deploys anyway). Homed alongside the composite-keying. Langston lean: persisted if low-cost else the stamp; CC-B: bundle with OBJ-5/6.** **★ ZERO-CODE DETECTION VERIFIED AIRTIGHT (Langston blind-spot check 2026-06-21): the `guard-eval-tracker` counter has exactly ONE zeroing that fires — `const _stats = new Map()` at module load (boot-coupled, once per process) — plus `resetGuardEvalStats()` (`_stats.clear()`) which has **ZERO callers** (grep-confirmed, dead utility), and there is no hot-reload (prod runs the built bundle). So counters can only zero at process boot ⟹ pm2 uptime ≥ window age PROVES the counters are intact. GUARD CONDITION: if any future change WIRES `resetGuardEvalStats` (or any other reset) onto a non-boot path, this airtightness breaks and the `trackerStartedAt` stamp becomes a MUST-HAVE (not the else-branch) for OBJ-5/6. Until then, zero-code detection holds.** **★ RESIDUAL (CC-B 2026-06-29, from resolving false-fire alert b0ba8e56): the scheduled window-validity CHECK that fires the "guard-eval window invalid" alert still uses the OLD pm2-uptime-vs-anchor logic (process uptime < 48h ⇒ "wiped"). That is now OBSOLETE — #374 OBJ-A DEPLOYED tracker persistence (checkpoint+reload), so the window survives restarts (verified live: `guard-eval-checkpoint.json` startedAt=2026-06-23T19:51:53Z survived ~14 restarts; reorg-B2.3 already calibrated minRR off this exact persisted window). The check FALSE-FIRED b0ba8e56 a 4th/5th time on process-uptime alone. FIX: the check must read the persisted `getGuardEvalStartedAt()` (now exposed) vs now, NOT process uptime. - **#376 — guard-eval-tracker `rrMin`/`rrMax` Infinity-sentinel round-trips to `null` over JSON (Langston Step-4 latent flag, reorg-B2.2 2026-06-23; HOME: reorg-B2.2 OBJ-C, the next tracker-touching sub-batch).** A bucket that records evals but never reaches the RR check (e.g. only `invalid_atr`/`stop_distance` drops) keeps `rrMin=Infinity`/`rrMax=-Infinity`, which `JSON.stringify` serializes to `null` (in the checkpoint AND the HTTP payload). **Consequential reload path is ALREADY GUARDED:** the tracker's reload restores the sentinels (`Number.isFinite(r.rrMin) ? r.rrMin : Infinity`) before any `recordGuardEval`, so the `Math.min(null, rr)→0` coercion Langston described does NOT occur on our reload. The only residual is the HTTP wire payload showing `null` for `rrMin/rrMax` — a field the UI does not render and the #372 read does not consume → **impact NIL today.** Pre-existing from reorg-B2.1's `_blank`/checkpoint shape, not introduced by the re-key. **FIX (fold into reorg-B2.2 OBJ-C — the next tracker-touching diff, NOT its own batch — Langston's call): serialize the Infinity sentinels explicitly (e.g. write `null` + normalize on read, or omit when sentinel) so the wire payload is honest.** **OPEN (reorg-B2.2 OBJ-C).**
- **#377 — xStock strong-trend signals reach the open-gate with NULL `dbs_score_at_queue` → 0.40-floor pWin (reorg-B3 FIND 2; CC-B + Langston Step-4 2026-06-24). HOME: the xStock active-path work (reorg-B8 "xStock net-new" / before xStock active-paper turn-on).** reorg-B3 threads the at-queue EV inputs from the crypto FX5 survivor snapshot (the pool the scanner populates). xStock does NOT flow through the crypto FX5 scanner, so `getFX5DataForSymbol` returns null for an xStock symbol → `dbs_score_at_queue`/`di_at_queue` persist NULL → at the open-gate a `quant-strong_trend` xStock signal pins the kernel strong-trend pWin at the 0.40 floor (fail-safe: lower pWin, fewer entries — no incorrect opens, but it gates out legitimate xStock strong-trend setups once xStock active-paper turns on). **This is a REAL fix, NOT an accepted permanent gap (Langston §13):** xStock HAS a per-class DBS (B79.0n.MCE) — it simply is not wired into the at-queue carrier. The fix = source xStock's per-class DBS into the at-queue carrier (a non-FX5 path for the xStock builder), landed before xStock active-paper turn-on. For crypto the same null path is provably UNREACHABLE (strong-trend requires |DBS|≥0.35, the exclusive lane at `canonical-regime-strategy-map.ts:1043/1080`, and only the scanner computes DBS → the floor handler is belt-and-suspenders there). **OPEN (reorg-B8 / xStock active-path).**

Deployed `c077faa7f`.
- **#379 — `/api/diagnostics/rtb-metrics` hand-maps a SUBSET of `getSummary()` → diagnostic fields silently dropped at the endpoint boundary (reorg-B3 deploy-verify finding + Langston NO-PATCHES flag 2026-06-24). HOME: a §13 scheduled refactor (verbatim-minus-redaction), my #370 block.** reorg-B3's OBJ-4 proof surface (`evInputThreadProof`) was added to `getSummary()` but did NOT reach the endpoint, because the route constructs its own subset object rather than returning `getSummary()` verbatim — caught by §9.3 deploy-verify, fixed for the instance in `99887f90e`. **But it's a defect CLASS, not a one-off:** `openFailedByStage` (a B6.5e field) is ALSO silently dropped by the same subset-map → the next diagnostic that needs a `getSummary()` field will hit this again. **FIX (durable): return `getSummary()` verbatim, then explicitly override ONLY the two intentional transforms — `byReason`→`byBlockReason` rename, `bySymbol`→count unless `?raw=1` — so no field is ever silently dropped again. This is a consumer-facing default-payload change → requires a byte-for-byte consumer diff (confirm existing keys unchanged) before landing.** Surface `openFailedByStage` in the same pass. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-24 (reorg-B3.1)** — rtb-metrics endpoint now returns `getSummary()` verbatim-minus-redaction; the subset-map silent-drop defect-class is closed. Deployed `c077faa7f`.
- **#380 ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-24 (reorg-B3.2) — VTS strangled by the reorg-B2 RR gate; tag-don't-drop fix (Kyle-surfaced; CC-B + Langston + Kyle consensus).** VTS opens collapsed 95-97% (~150/day → 3-5/day, staging `vts_open_trades`) from the reorg-B2 `min_rr=2.5` gate wired into vts-runner (suppresses 62-100% per #372). - **#381 — rtb-metrics endpoint flipped allowlist→denylist (reorg-B3.1 #379 forward-note; Langston flag, 2026-06-24).** #379 made `/api/diagnostics/rtb-metrics` spread `getSummary()` (verbatim-minus-redaction) instead of hand-picking fields. - **#382 — reorg-B3.3x: xStock VTS un-strangle (tag-don't-drop at the xStock eval-cycle gate chain) — the §13 home for the Option-A fast-follow (CC-B + Langston 2026-06-24). HOME: the IMMEDIATE next batch after reorg-B3.3 (named, not "fast-follow").** reorg-B3.3 built the SHARED strategy `gateDisposition` param for BOTH classes and opted the CRYPTO VTS path into `'tag'` (composes with reorg-B3.2's downstream normalizer → fully working + verifiable). **xStock was deliberately left default-`'enforce'` (still strangled) because its downstream is STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT:** the xStock eval-cycle (`server/asset_classes/xstock_spot/eval-cycle.ts:526`) has NO `normalizeAndGateTarget` (reorg-B3.2 never ran there) to re-derive + tag the verdict, and it has its OWN Net-EV-floor gate (`:657`, `netEV ≤ VTS_NET_EV_FLOOR` → SQE reject). So opting eval-cycle into `'tag'` alone would (a) push some signals to the EV floor which still drops them, and (b) capture no `vtsGateVerdict`. **reorg-B3.3x = give xStock its own tag-don't-drop disposition + `vtsGateVerdict` capture at the eval-cycle gate chain (mirroring what reorg-B3.2 did for crypto), then opt eval-cycle:526 into `'tag'`.** Precedent: reorg-B3.2 itself did crypto-only on the downstream side. **xStock VTS stays strangled until B3.3x lands — keep the window short; B3.3x is the very next batch off the rank after reorg-B3.3 closes.** ↔ #380, reorg-B3.3, reorg-B2.3. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-24 (reorg-B3.3x — Langston Option B: UNIFY onto the shared normalizer, NOT a hand-rolled second copy). eval-cycle:526 opts into `'tag'` + runs the SAME `normalizeAndGateTarget` (after detect, before the Net-EV floor): quality TAG+simulate, validity DROP (xStock GAINED the B3.3y `target<=entry` backstop for free). `vtsGateVerdict` threaded onto the shared `OpenVirtualTrade` record via `RegisterOpenVtsTradeInput` (Langston Step-1 catch — the input lacked the field). So the normalizer is now the ONE shared VTS gate for both classes. Step-7: xStock TAG_NO_DROP markers fire (KR/MRNA/MRVL). Staging `53f601b93` restart#415, CI `28131918663`, no migration. In-memory-only verdict gap → #384→reorg-B4.**
  - **★ KNOWN-BENIGN, INTENTIONALLY-LEFT (reorg-B3.3 Step-4, CC-B + Langston 2026-06-24 — logged so a future reader doesn't re-surface it as a bug):** the reachability gate uses DIFFERENT ATR denominators in the two implementations — the strategy guard (`applyGlobalGuards`) uses `clampEffectiveATR` (ATR capped at `price×0.10`), the downstream normalizer (`normalizeAndGateTarget`, vts-runner:1189) uses RAW `mceContext.indicators.atr`. Since `clamped ≤ raw`, the strategy guard is strictly STRICTER on reachability, so "strategy passes reach but normalizer fails" is impossible. Label-neutral on both paths: under `'enforce'` the stricter strategy bound is the pre-existing active gate and the lenient normalizer can't contradict it downward; under `'tag'` the strategy guard never decides the label (`unreachable` is taggable → it continues), and the normalizer is AUTHORITATIVE for the recorded `vtsGateVerdict`. Pre-existing from reorg-B2.1 (guard) vs reorg-B2 (normalizer); aligning the normalizer call to clamped ATR is out-of-scope churn with no behavioral payoff → **deliberately NOT aligned.** (This is the same divergence #373 tracks for the OBJ-5 normalizer-retirement read; when the normalizer retires, the divergence dissolves.)
- **#383 — reorg-B3.3y: symmetric `invalid_geometry` — drop `target<=entry` / `reward<=0` longs (validity, not quality) (CC-B surfaced live + Langston Step-8 call 2026-06-24). HOME: its OWN tiny batch reorg-B3.3y, sequenced in the reorg-B3.3 family.** reorg-B3.3 (the VTS un-strangle) EXPOSED a pre-existing validity gap: `normalizeAndGateTarget`'s geometry guard (`signal-target-normalizer.ts:73-78`) is ASYMMETRIC — it nets `stop>=entry` (no risk leg) as `invalid_geometry` but MISSES `target<=entry` (no reward leg). So a long with `target≤entry` (reward ≤ 0) computes `rr ≤ 0 < minRR` → falls into the **`rr_below_min` QUALITY-tag bucket** and, on the VTS `'tag'` path, gets simulated instead of dropped. **Live evidence:** `USDT/GBP/volatility_edge would-gate=rr_below_min rr=-0.00 — simulating anyway` has fired ~1/min since 2026-06-24 17:59Z. **Why it's wrong (Langston):** the `rr_below_min` tag bucket exists to learn "do positive-but-sub-threshold reward:risk trades pay net of friction?" — a `reward≤0` long can NEVER pay; it is degenerate geometry (Net-Expectancy noise by definition), not a low-quality-but-valid trade, so simulating it (a) answers no question the bucket asks and (b) biases the `rr_below_min` cohort's own stats downward with non-members. **FIX = make `invalid_geometry` SYMMETRIC: a valid long requires `stop < entry < target`; add the `target<=entry` (reward-leg) check → `invalid_geometry` DROP on every path** (mirrors the existing `stop>=entry` check + a unit test pinning `target<=entry → invalid_geometry DROP`). One-condition surgical fix on the CRYPTO normalizer's validity check — **kept SEPARATE from reorg-B3.3x** (that's the xStock eval-cycle gate chain, a different code site; folding muddies two clean scopes — Langston). **Interim contamination is BOUNDED** (the downstream VTS Net-EV floor rejects a reward≤0 trade from opening as a win), so it's a named follow-up, not a hold on B3.3 closure. **Sequence:** Langston recommends right behind reorg-B3.3x; CC-B may pull it AHEAD since it's actively polluting + trivial — decide at the time. ↔ reorg-B3.3, #382. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-24 (reorg-B3.3y, pulled AHEAD of B3.3x per Kyle 2026-06-24 + Langston's OK) — `normalizeAndGateTarget` geometry guard made SYMMETRIC: added `nativeTarget <= entryPrice` so a valid long requires `stop < entry < target`; a reward≤0 long now returns `invalid_geometry` (validity DROP on every path) instead of `rr_below_min` (quality tag). Active path unchanged (already dropped it as rr_below_min; now drops as invalid_geometry — only the log label moves; verified orchestrator:1232 drops on any !ok). VTS moves it tag→validity-drop. New test `reorg-b3-3y` 5/5; tsc baseline OK; no migration. Langston Step-4 CONCUR. Commit `2136c74fe`.**
- **#384 — `vtsGateVerdict` is IN-MEMORY-ONLY (no DB column) → the "what would active do" filter is queryable on LIVE opens but NOT closed/historical, for BOTH crypto + xStock (surfaced reorg-B3.3x, Langston Step-4 2026-06-24). HOME: reorg-B4 (the shadow-trade DATA-ENGINE / telemetry batch).** reorg-B3.2 added `vtsGateVerdict` to the in-memory `OpenVirtualTrade` record (crypto inline ~vts-runner:1649; xStock via `registerOpenVtsTrade` from reorg-B3.3x) but deliberately took NO migration (re-derivable from geometry). NOT needed for the B3.3/B3.3x un-strangle itself (which works on the live path). ↔ reorg-B3.2, reorg-B3.3x, reorg-B4. **OPEN (homed to reorg-B4).**
- **#385 — DECISION (Kyle + CC-B + Langston consensus 2026-06-24): KEEP the VTS Net-EV floor ENFORCING — do NOT extend tag-don't-drop to it. The tabled "tag-don't-drop the EV floor" question has a NAMED, MEASURABLE revisit trigger (HOME: post-reorg-B7).** Post-reorg-B3.3 staging read: the un-strangle works at the strategy level (crypto opens 3-5/day → ~28/24h; xStock ~30/24h), but the bottleneck moved DOWN to the **Net-EV floor** — crypto **2,206 signals rejected `netEvBelowFloor` vs 28 generated** (~99% EV-reject) because the realistic ~1.9% Tier-1 friction makes most crypto signals net-negative. **The question:** extend the reorg-B3.2 tag-don't-drop to the VTS Net-EV floor (capture negative-EV counterfactuals) OR keep it enforcing? **DECISION = keep ENFORCING.** Rationale: (a) **Kyle's diagnostic-preservation point** — the crypto EV-pass rate (2206/28) is a clean before/after INSTRUMENT for measuring reorg-B7's maker/taker lift; loosening now would MASK it. (b) **Langston: the circularity logic does NOT transfer** — tag-don't-drop on the RR gate was justified because that gate filtered on uncertain *self-referential model outputs* (pre-filtering the data you'd validate it with); the Net-EV floor filters on *realized economics* (~1.9% known external friction) — net-negative is noise *by construction*, not circular. So enforcing is consistent, not an exception. (c) ~~xStock already flows EV-clean~~ **[CORRECTED 2026-06-25 — this basis was WRONG; xStock is fee-wall-affected at the SAME rate as crypto, #386 RETRACTED via #387 counter bug].** The decision STILL HOLDS on (a)+(b) alone: keeping the floor enforcing for both classes preserves the maker/taker diagnostic for both and is the consistent realized-economics call; the data-starvation concern is moot because the floor was never going to be loosened. **REVISIT TRIGGER (explicit + measurable):** *post-reorg-B7, measure the crypto `netEvBelowFloor` pass-rate delta; reopen the tag-don't-drop-the-EV-floor question ONLY IF (a) maker/taker fails to move crypto net-EV meaningfully positive AND (b) a named friction-model-calibration need exists.* **TELEMETRY CAVEAT (Langston — "table ≠ discard"):** confirm/enrich the `netEvBelowFloor` rejection logging to carry count-by-(strategy×regime) + the EV-shortfall DISTRIBUTION (how far below floor, not a boolean) — gives the precise reorg-B7 baseline + a mine-able rejection ledger (without ever promoting negative-EV signals into VTS as trades). HOME the telemetry enrichment as **reorg-B7 prep** (or a small near-term batch if convenient). ↔ #386, reorg-B7, Phase-25. **OPEN (decision MADE; revisit gated on reorg-B7 + the 2 conditions).**
- **#386 — ❌ RETRACTED 2026-06-25 (the "crypto-vs-xStock EV asymmetry" finding was WRONG — built on a buggy counter; see #387).** ORIGINAL CLAIM (2026-06-24): crypto fee-wall-bound but xStock clears the EV floor at current fees (`netEvBelowFloor=0`) → "xStock fee-work is low priority." **THIS IS FALSE.** The `xstocks/filter-diagnostics` endpoint reports `netEvBelowFloor=0` due to a COUNTER BUG (#387); the `signal_eval_archive` SSOT shows xStock **DOES** get EV-rejected at essentially the SAME rate as crypto — **2-hour window: crypto `sqe/net_ev_rejected`=593 vs xStock `sqe/net_ev_below_floor`=611.** So BOTH classes are fee-wall-affected similarly (identical 0.8% taker fees → both reject ~99% of EV-stage signals). **CORRECTED sequencing implication:** maker/taker switching (reorg-B7) matters for **BOTH** classes, NOT crypto-only; there is NO basis to deprioritize xStock fee-work. The lesson (Kyle caught it): do NOT trust the filter-diagnostics endpoint counters — use `signal_eval_archive` (the persisted decision SSOT). ↔ #385, #387, reorg-B7. **RETRACTED.**
- **#387 — ✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-25 (B-DIAG-387, deployed staging `1c451f5b5` restart#416, CI 4-green `28161070581`; Langston Step-4 APPROVED). The `xstocks/filter-diagnostics` endpoint under-reported EV-floor rejections — `rejectedReasons.netEvBelowFloor` showed 0 forever while the real count was ~611/2h.** ROOT CAUSE: the endpoint (`routes.ts:7846`) read `byReason['net_ev_below_floor'] || totalRejected` — `byReason` was permanently-empty "reference shape" scaffolding (never populated) and `totalRejected` stayed 0, so the tile was hard-zero. The xStock eval-cycle (`xstock_spot/eval-cycle.ts:716`) rejected on `netEV ≤ VTS_NET_EV_FLOOR` + archived it (`reject_stage='sqe'`, `reason='net_ev_below_floor'`) + bumped `signalsRejectedBySQE`, but recorded the reason in no in-memory counter the endpoint read. **This MISLED a decision-grade read (#386 retracted).** **FIX (observability-only — no gate/strategy/regime/signal-pipeline/EV-math change):** (OBJ-1) the reject site now writes `net_ev_rejected` into `nullReasonAggregate` + the per-lane aggregates; the endpoint sources `rejectedReasons.netEvBelowFloor` from the lifetime accumulator (`lt.nullReasonAggregate['net_ev_rejected']`), parity with the crypto vts-runner path. (OBJ-2, no-hidden-gates) the 3 previously-invisible pre-open/TCL gate reasons (`reentry_cooldown`, `price_past_stop`, `price_past_target`) are now surfaced in the structured `nullReasons` + 3 new panel rows (per-lane accurate). (§18) the dead `byReason`/`totalRejected`/`byRegime`/`total*`/`byStrategy` scaffolding + the always-empty `signalRejections` field were excised (DELETED_COMPONENTS_LOG B-DIAG-387). **PROOF (exact, per Langston Step-4 cond 4):** live endpoint `netEvBelowFloor` == `signal_eval_archive` `net_ev_below_floor` count over the matched since-restart window = **25 == 25, exact** (no skew) — confirms the scanner lifetime-merge carries the new key + the single reject site is 1:1 with the archive. ↔ #385, #386, reorg-B4, reorg-B7. **RESOLVED.**
- **#391 OPEN 2026-06-26 (P19-B6.6 Step-2 / Langston conditions — HOME = B7b activation) — post-B7b: monitor the xStock price-discovery-liveness in-RTH `flat_last` block-rate (window-tuning signal) + alert on block-rate spike / config-null.** B6.6 pins the liveness window at 45 min from 3 weekday sessions of archived data; the active path is DORMANT so the live block-rate can't be observed until B7b turns xStock active-paper on. Two post-B7b observability needs: **(1)** measure the in-RTH `flat_last` block-rate per symbol — if EWN/EWP/TOTL-class deep-but-slow ETF tokens false-block more than expected, that is the evidence to widen the (tier-extensible) `price_discovery_liveness.window_ms` config (or add a per-tier override row); **(2)** the gate is fail-closed, so a `module_constants`/DB hiccup that resolves the config to `null` silently blocks ALL xStock fills (right fail-direction, but invisible) — wire an alert when the liveness block-rate spikes OR the config resolves null, so a feed/config outage doesn't masquerade as a market state; **(3)** GOTU-class loose-admission watch — GOTU (strong_trend-only admission, ~$2.3K depth, ~33m worst-day in-RTH p99) PASSES the 45m window at only 1.36× margin (vs ≥2× for the active cluster), the one admitted name not excluded by the window; watch GOTU-class names admitted at <2× window margin for worst-day stale fills, and if observed the fix is the CONDITIONING path (tighten `strong_trend` `lq_min`/depth), NOT moving the global liveness window (Langston Step-2 GOTU disposition; the residual is already bounded by the open-seam depth-sufficiency gate); **(4)** the windowed `last`-move read's hard timeout is a JS-side **`Promise.race`** that rejects at `query_timeout_ms` but does NOT cancel the underlying `db.execute` — so a timed-out query keeps running server-side (orphaned). Fail direction is correct (timeout → block) and harmless at per-open frequency (the depth gate has NO timeout at all, so liveness is strictly more defensive), but the orphaned-query pattern under a sustained-timeout storm is connection-pool pressure. **Disposition = ACCEPTED for B6.6 (academic while dormant); revisit trigger (post-B7b) = if the liveness-block-rate monitoring above ever shows a timeout cluster + pool pressure, replace `Promise.race` with a transaction-scoped `SET LOCAL statement_timeout` that actually CANCELS the query** (Langston Step-4 §13 named-home ask). The `flat_last`/`no_data`/`sparse_snapshots`/`liveness_timeout` reason codes (B6.6 OBJ-4, into `recordDepthGateBlock`) are the data source. **HOME (CONCRETE): the B7b activation pre-flight/first-48h observation list** (PHASE_19_PLAN §6). - **#395 OPEN 2026-06-26 (Langston B6.6 Step-8 finding — LIVE on restart#420; CC-B confirmed 64,494 occurrences) — the crypto VTS strategy-detect path throws `ReferenceError: setNullReason is not defined`, silently starving the VTS learning stream.** Every failure logs `[11.0E.1][VTS] Strategy execution failed for <PAIR>: ReferenceError: setNullReason is not defined` at `callStrategyDetect` inside `generatePhase10Signal`/`runPhase10SimulationCycle` (bundle offset, drifted 34313→34492 across builds = **longstanding, persisted across multiple deploys**). **Confirmed live (CC-B, restart#420):** `grep -c setNullReason ~/.pm2/logs/dawntrader-error.log` = **64,494** (== `Strategy execution failed` count, so EVERY VTS detect failure is this ReferenceError), across a large set of crypto pairs (AAVE/ADA·multi-quote/AERO/AVAX/CVX/SPX/TRX/…/USELESS, latest USELESS/USD). **NOT a source bug:** `setNullReason` IS imported (`strategy-engine.ts:22`, `vts-runner.ts:198`) and present in the bundle (148 defs) — it throws ReferenceError at the bundled call site = an **esbuild scope / circular-import signature** (the binding isn't in lexical scope at that call site in the bundled output; likely a temporal-dead-zone / circular-init ordering from the reorg-B2/B3/B3.3 `setNullReason`-threading-through-18-strategies work). **NOT B6.6's doing** (B6.6's 6 files don't touch the detect path; `git log` on those files points at the reorg series; the error predates B6.6). **WHY IT MATTERS (decision-grade):** we're in VTS/passive-learning mode — every thrown detect → that pair logs **no VTS signal/learning record that cycle** → silently starving the Net-Expectancy-relevant learning data stream (not log noise). **HOME (CC-B + Langston consensus 2026-06-26): a dedicated diagnostic/fix batch that PREEMPTS B6.7** — a live learning-data-integrity bug outranks #301's cosmetic 2nd-WS cleanup. The existing tsc-baseline gate already catches a missing-import regression. **★SEPARATE finding homed → #396** (error.log also held ~35k CURRENT feed errors). ↔ #396, B6.7. | RESOLVED (historical; no live bug; log cleared; durable refactor recommended-scheduled)
- **#396 OPEN 2026-06-26 (surfaced during the #395 error-log review — SEPARATE current feed/scanner issue, NOT historical) — the market-data WS + adaptive scanner throw a steady stream of `Sub Error: Method(s) not found`, `Data stale`, and `Underfilled batch` errors.** While clearing the stale `dawntrader-error.log` for #395, its composition showed — beyond the 64,494 historical setNullReason — **~35k CURRENT errors: `[8.9.0-B][WS] Sub Error: Method(s) not found` (14,630), `[MD-WS] Data stale` (10,177), `[11.4C-R2][AdaptiveScan] Underfilled batch: 43/300` (9,849).** Unlike #395 these are NOT a build artifact: "Method(s) not found" is a Kraken WS reply to a `subscribe` call with a wrong method/params, and it correlates with the intermittent stale-data + the underfilled scanner batches (43/300). **POSSIBLE TIE-IN:** during the #395 review, VTS cycles showed long stretches of "Regime Dist: TREND=0,RANGE=0,HIVOL=0 | 0 trades simulated" alongside these stale-data warnings — i.e. when the feed goes stale, no fresh prices reach the strategies → no regimes → no signals. VTS DOES still produce trades intermittently (Cycle 80/84/161/236 had 1–2) with a healthy pool (165–225), so the feed is INTERMITTENTLY degraded, not dead — but a recurring WS-subscription error that periodically starves the scanner is a real learning-throughput drag worth root-causing (which `subscribe` is failing + why the batch underfills to 43/300). **NOT investigated yet — observation only.** **HOME (CONCRETE): a dedicated feed-health diagnostic batch** (candidate `B-FEED-WS-SUBERR`), sequence TBD with Kyle/Langston — likely after the B6 series unless it proves to materially starve VTS learning, in which case it preempts. Surfaced by CC-B 2026-06-26. **★ SPLIT + PARTLY-RESOLVED 2026-06-26 (P19-B6.7 pre-audit):** the two WS-error classes were the **§15-DELETED vestigial 2nd WS**, not the real feed — **`[8.9.0-B][WS] Sub Error` (14.6k) = HISTORICAL** (pre-#300, when the 2nd WS still drove subscribes; the current build had 0 `subscribeToPair` callers so it sent none), **`[MD-WS] Data stale` (10.2k) = the dead 2nd-WS heartbeat** (30s heartbeat vs 2s threshold) which P19-B6.7 **structurally REMOVED** (Langston-verified gone post-restart#421). So the WS half is RESOLVED-by-removal. **The scanner `Underfilled batch 43/300` (9.8k) is the genuinely-separate REMAINING issue:** `market-scanner.ts` refill (~:600) tops up from `allPairs`, so it can't rescue a SHORT Kraken universe fetch (`getTicker()`⋈`getTradablePairs()` filtered by `pairInfo`) — P19-B6.7 shipped PURE TELEMETRY (OBJ-4) attributing which call is short + the join-drop count. **THE FIX is homed → `P19-B6.9`** (after the telemetry attributes the live cause: transient REST hiccup vs join-filter drop). Updated CC-B 2026-06-26. **★ PARKED 2026-06-30 (P19-B6.9) — NOT reproducing, scheduled-on-evidence (§13; CC-B + Langston consensus).** Verified at B6.9 Step-2: the deployed OBJ-4 telemetry (`market-scanner.ts:572-585`, marker `[AdaptiveScan][11.4C.1][#396] SHORT UNIVERSE`, fires only when `krakenUniverseSize < BATCH_SIZE`) is **SILENT across a 12k-line staging window**; the Kraken universe fetch is stable ~1506, the scanner evaluates 342-361/cycle, and NO "Underfilled batch" lines appear. (The "Available=1 → rotational expanded to 299" lines are the normal batch-composition backfill path — candidate-quality, NOT a short fetch.) So the short-universe condition is not occurring; building a fix now would be speculative (and the telemetry can't attribute a cause that isn't firing). **CONCRETE RE-TRIGGER (the §13 home):** reopen + fix the moment the deployed `[#396] SHORT UNIVERSE` telemetry FIRES — the marker attributes the cause (getTicker-short / getTradablePairs-short / pairInfo-join-drops), which dictates the targeted fix. The telemetry IS the canary; this is scheduled-on-evidence, not unscheduled-watch. | PARKED (P19-B6.9 — telemetry-evidence + `[#396] SHORT UNIVERSE` re-trigger)
- **#398 OPEN 2026-06-26 (Langston P19-B6.7 Step-4 latent flag — §13 home) — `parity-gate.assessWsReadiness` derives WS uptime from the primary adapter's CUMULATIVE `reconnectAttempts` over a fixed ~120-check denominator, so a long-lived process can accrete enough lifetime reconnects to push `uptimePercent` below the 99% floor and spuriously FAIL the Phase-21 go-live WS gate even on a perfectly fresh feed.** The vestigial 2nd-WS coordinator effectively never reconnected (always read ~100%), masking this; the PRIMARY adapter genuinely reconnects in production, so the cumulative-counter/fixed-denominator math drifts downward over uptime. Not urgent — parity-gate is dormant until Phase-21 go-live (no active gating today) — but a real trap to fix BEFORE go-live: re-base the counter per-window (interval reconnects, like the feed-integrity monitor now does) or compute uptime over a rolling window, not lifetime. **HOME → `P19-B6.9`** (alongside the #396 scanner fix — both are pre-Phase-21 go-live-path hardening). Surfaced by Langston (P19-B6.7 Step-4), homed by CC-B 2026-06-26. **★ RESOLVED 2026-06-30 (P19-B6.9).** Re-based `assessWsReadiness` (`feed-health-aggregate.ts`) off a ROLLING 1h window via the new pure `computeRollingWindowReadiness(reconnectsPerInterval[], minSamples)` (denominator = snapshots PRESENT in the feed-integrity ring, capped at 12 — NOT intervals-since-boot), fed by the new `feedIntegrityMonitor.getRollingWindowReadiness()` getter; the cumulative-`reconnectAttempts`/fixed-120 formula is REMOVED (not stubbed). `MIN_READINESS_SAMPLES=6` (30 min) fail-closed warm-up below floor (`uptimePercent:null` → gate not-ready, never a misleading %). Regression test pins the exact failure (high lifetime reconnects + clean recent window → PASS). The 99%-floor-vs-12-snapshot-granularity interaction (1 reconnect/hr → 91.7% → fails) is a Phase-21 THRESHOLD-calibration item, NOT the drift bug — flagged at POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP §3.5 item 21-3b (B6.9 fixes the drift, not the floor). Head `c1e5ef80c`, Langston Step-4 APPROVED (delta-semantics confirmed), 37/37 tests. | RESOLVED (P19-B6.9)
- **#397 OPEN 2026-06-26 (CC-B + Langston consensus — governance-checker hygiene; THREE identical false-burst incidents = structural) — the governance-checker grades a batch's doc-set the instant its CLOSING COMMIT is detectable at origin, before the batch's governance-doc commits are guaranteed present, producing a false "missing required doc" alert burst at every architecture-batch close.** Signature seen THREE times now (B-GOV-4, B-TEC, P19-B6.6): the checker fires 6–7 `Missing required governance doc` alerts (+ an `undeclared change-class` alert) within minutes of close; the docs land a few commits later; the alerts never auto-clear and have to be hand-resolved. For P19-B6.6 the burst fired 00:08Z, the docs+corrected-change-class were already pushed by ~00:0x–02:37Z, and a checker re-run on staging vs origin reports `class=architecture declared:true, missingRequired:[], pre-audit citesSim/citesManual + 10 file:line cites` — i.e. zero real gap. **ROOT CAUSE (not a tuning miss): "batch is closed" is being INFERRED from the wrong signal — the closing/code commit — when "closed" and "docset-present" are two different events that the push ordering lets separate.** **DURABLE FIX (Langston rec, §8 #11 no-patches — do NOT just widen the grace/deadline timer, which only relocates the race): grade on a COMPLETION SENTINEL that by construction cannot exist before the docset does** — e.g. trigger grading on the completion-report file landing (or an explicit close-marker the implementer emits AFTER governance lands, or an atomic docs+change-class+close-marker single push) — so doc-presence IS the trigger and there is no window to tune. **INTERIM mitigation only (NOT the fix, must not be logged as done): push the governance-doc commits BEFORE the closing/change-class commit** so the docset is already at origin when close becomes detectable. **HOME: a checker-hygiene batch in CC-B's #370–399 range** (the poller has a deadline timer — confirm whether it grades on close-commit detection vs a sentinel; the fix lives in `scripts/governance-checker/poller.mjs` + `checker.mjs`). Until it ships, stale bursts get hand-resolved with a staging checker-core verification (the method used to clear the B6.6 set). Surfaced + verified by CC-B, framing ratified by Langston, 2026-06-26. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-06-26 by B-GOV-4** — ABSORBED from CC-B's #370–399 range into B-GOV-4 with CC-B's consent (same two files as #350; one diff avoided a shared-file collision). The durable fix landed exactly as framed: the doc-set gap now triggers on the completion-report SENTINEL (`hasCompletionReport`, set from the report's first-ADD), NOT the closing/code commit — so doc-presence is the trigger and there is no race window; plus order-independent auto-resolve (a slightly-early report's gap self-clears when the trailing doc lands) and an orphan re-verify sweep (a stranded alert is re-checked at GOV_REF, resolved only if genuinely satisfied — never blind-resolved). The interim "push docs before close" mitigation is moot. (Re-homed from CC-B → B-GOV-4.) | RESOLVED
- **#399 OPEN 2026-06-30 (P19-B7.1 ranking fix — the FIVE Phase-25 §13 homes, umbrella; CC-B + Langston + CC-A, scope-signed-off).** B7.1 ships the R-multiple ranker + its shadow/selection-IC + clamp-bind instrumentation NOW on CRUDE DI-derived pWin + CURRENT taker friction; five things are deliberately data-gated to Phase-25 and each is homed here so none becomes an open loop (§9.4). NOTE: CC-B's #370–399 reservation range is EXHAUSTED by this entry — revisit/extend the range (per the #400 convention) before the next CC-B issue. **(a) pWin CALIBRATION pipeline (the make-or-break)** — the DI→pWin map is uncalibrated; build base-rates→Platt/isotonic→reliability/Brier→shrink-to-base-rate, regime-conditioned, on LIVE shadow outcomes (NOT VTS breadth). HOME: Phase-25 (POST_AUDIT_ROADMAP §3.5 calibration-study set 25-12/13/14/15). Until it lands the cross-class comparison is provisional (within-class rank ~preserved; cross-class NOT). **(b) FRACTIONAL-KELLY sizing** — rank-by-R and the current fixed-fractional-risk sizer are on different bases; size by fractional Kelly keyed to calibrated-pWin confidence once (a) lands. HOME: Phase-25 (after the pWin calibration). **(c) xStock dbsScore-gap closure** — xStock strong-trend reaches the kernel with NULL `dbs_score_at_queue` → 0.40 pWin floor (`pwin_floored` is captured in the shadow layer); a HARD PREREQUISITE for xStock LIVE enablement (crude is fine for paper, not live). HOME: Phase-25/xStock (close before the xStock live gate). **(d) selection-IC GO/NO-GO** — run `core/metrics/selection-ic.ts` on accumulated post-switch-on shadow data: per-cycle cross-sectional Spearman + window-clustered SE + per-regime-family + min-N+CI + §19.4 sibling-control on CLEAN windows; a genuinely-positive selection IC is the proof the ranker beats friction (one-pick-per-cycle is low-breadth — Grinold). HOME: Phase-25 (the empirical ranker-validation gate; data accrues from B8 switch-on). **(e) per-class pWin HAIRCUT decision** — whether to apply a symmetric per-class pWin shrink until calibration lands (deferred deliberately — a pre-calibration haircut is itself an uncalibrated guess; Langston Q2); decide WITH the calibration data. HOME: Phase-25 (fold into (a)). All five appear in the B7.1 completion report's §13 section with these homes named. Surfaced + homed by CC-B, ratified by Langston (scope sign-off) + CC-A 2026-06-30. | OPEN
